whose reduced image is a rational normal curve. Moreover,
f
is a closed embedding if and only if
C
is not a hyperelliptic ribbon.

3.3.
Stacks of hyperelliptic curves and ribbons

Definition 3.11
.

Let
S
be a scheme. A
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration

P/S
is a morphism
P\to S
from a scheme
P
that is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle in the étale topology. In other words, a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration
P/S
is precisely a Severi–Brauer
S
-scheme
P
of relative dimension
1
.

Definition 3.12
.

We define the functors
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}
from locally Noetherian
\mathbbm{k}
-schemes to groupoids as

\mathscr{H}_{g}(S):=\left\{(f:X\to S)\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}(S)\left|\begin{array}[]{l}\textrm{there exists a $\mathbb{P}^{1}$-fibration $\varpi:P\to S$ and a }\\
\textrm{finite morphism $\varphi:X\to P$ such that $\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}$}\\
\textrm{is locally free of rank $2$ and $f=\varpi\circ\varphi$.}\end{array}\right.\right\}.

\mathscr{R}_{g}(S):=\left\{(f:X\to S)\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}(S)\left|\begin{array}[]{l}\textrm{there exists a $\mathbb{P}^{1}$-fibration $\varpi:P\to S$ and a}\\
\textrm{closed immersion $\psi:P\hookrightarrow X$ with ideal sheaf}\\
\textrm{$\mathcal{N}$ such that $\mathcal{N}^{2}=0$, $\mathcal{N}$ is a locally free}\\
\textrm{$\mathcal{O}_{P}$-module of rank $1$, and $\varpi=f\circ\psi$.}\end{array}\right.\right\}.

An object in
\mathscr{H}_{g}(S)
(resp.
\mathscr{R}_{g}(S)
) is called a
family of hyperelliptic curves
(resp. a
family of ribbons
) of genus
g
over
S
.

Remark 3.13
.

(1)

By Theorem
3.3
(resp. Definition
3.7
), we know that
\mathscr{H}_{g}(\mathbbm{k})
(resp.
\mathscr{R}_{g}(\mathbbm{k})
) is precisely the groupoid of all hyperelliptic curves (resp. all ribbons) of genus
g
.

(2)

Note that we assume the
\mathbbm{k}
-schemes
S
in the above definition to be locally Noetherian, as
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
is a locally Noetherian algebraic stack. One can define the functors over arbitrary
\mathbbm{k}
-schemes by bootstrapping from the locally Noethrian case, see e.g.
[
ABB+23
, Definition 3.4]
.

(3)

In the above definition, we only assume the existence of a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration
P/S
, while no specific choice of
P/S
is taken. Nevertheless, as we shall see in proofs of Theorems
3.14
and
3.15
, the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration
P/S
is indeed unique up to unique isomorphism once exists. The advantage of our definition is that there are natural monomorphisms of stacks from
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}
to
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
, which turn out to be locally closed immersions (see Proposition
4.5
).

Theorem 3.14
.

The functor
\mathscr{H}_{g}
is represented by an algebraic stack of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
. Moreover, we have an isomorphism of stacks

\mathscr{H}_{g}\cong[\mathbb{A}(H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(2g+2)))/(\mathrm{GL}(2)/\bm{\mu}_{g+1})],

where the
\mathrm{GL}(2)/\bm{\mu}_{g+1}
-action is induced by the standard isomorphism of
\mathrm{GL}(2)
with the automorphism group
\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1))
.

Proof.

First of all, we show that
\mathscr{H}_{g}
is a prestack, i.e. a category fibered in groupoids. Suppose
(f:X\to S)\in\mathscr{H}_{g}(S)
. Let
g:S^{\prime}\to S
be a morphism of locally Noetherian
\mathbbm{k}
-schemes. Then we have
f^{\prime}:X^{\prime}=X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
as the base change of
f
under
g
. Clearly,
f^{\prime}
is the pull-back of
f
under
g
in the stack
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
. Thus it suffices to show that
f^{\prime}\in\mathscr{H}_{g}(S^{\prime})
. Let
\varpi^{\prime}:P^{\prime}=P\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
and
\varphi^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\to P^{\prime}
be the base change of
\varpi
and
\varphi
under
g
respectively. Then clearly
P^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration, and
\varphi^{\prime}
is a finite morphism such that
f^{\prime}=\varpi^{\prime}\circ\varphi^{\prime}
. Denote by
g_{P}:P^{\prime}\to P
the base change of
g
under
\varpi
. Since
\varphi
is affine, by
[
Sta25
,
Tag 02KG
]
we have that
\varphi^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}=g_{P}^{*}\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
is locally free of rank
2
. Thus
f^{\prime}\in\mathscr{H}_{g}(S^{\prime})
.

Next, we define a functor
\mathscr{H}_{g}^{\prime}
from locally Noetherian
\mathbbm{k}
-schemes to groupoids as

\mathscr{H}_{g}^{\prime}(S):=\left\{(P/S,\mathcal{L},i:\mathcal{L}^{\otimes 2}\to\mathcal{O}_{P})\left|\begin{array}[]{l}P/S\textrm{ is a $\mathbb{P}^{1}$-fibration, $\mathcal{L}$ is a line bundle on $P$}\\
\textrm{whose restriction to every geometric fiber has}\\
\textrm{degree $-g-1$, and $i$ is a homomorphism of}\\
\textrm{$\mathcal{O}_{P}$-modules.}\end{array}\right.\right\}.

Note that our definition of
\mathscr{H}_{g}^{\prime}
is very similar to
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}(1,2,g+1)
from
[
AV04
, Remark 3.3]
, except that we allow
i
to be zero on fibers. By the same argument as
[
AV04
, Proof of Theorem 4.1]
, we have that
\mathscr{H}_{g}^{\prime}
is a stack and

(3.1)

\mathscr{H}_{g}^{\prime}\cong[\mathbb{A}(H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(2g+2)))/(\mathrm{GL}(2)/\bm{\mu}_{g+1})].

Next, we show that
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{H}_{g}^{\prime}
are equivalent as fibered categories. Let
F:\mathscr{H}_{g}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{H}_{g}
be a functor that sends
(P/S,\mathcal{L},i)\in\mathscr{H}_{g}^{\prime}(S)
to
(X\to S)\in\mathscr{H}_{g}(S)
, where

X:=\Spec_{P}\mathcal{O}_{P}\oplus\mathcal{L},

and the algebra structure on
\mathcal{O}_{P}\oplus\mathcal{L}
is determined by
i:\mathcal{L}^{\otimes 2}\to\mathcal{O}_{P}
. It is not hard to see that
X\to S
is a family of hyperelliptic curves.

To show that
F
is an equivalence of fibered categories, it suffices to show that
F
is fully faithful and essentially surjective.
We start from showing the essential surjectivity of
F
.
Let
(\pi:X\to S)\in\mathscr{H}_{g}(S)
be a family of hyperelliptic curves.

Let
\mathcal{F}:=\ker(\mathcal{O}_{P}\to\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X})
and
\mathcal{L}:=\mathrm{coker}(\mathcal{O}_{P}\to\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X})
. For every point
s\in S
, by Theorem
3.3
we know that there is an exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{s}}\to\varphi_{s,*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}\to\mathcal{L}_{s}\to 0

where
\mathcal{L}_{s}
is a line bundle on
P_{s}
. Since
\varphi_{s,*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}
is the pull-back of
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
by
[
Sta25
,
Tag 02KG
]
, we know that
\mathcal{L}_{s}
is also the pull-back of
\mathcal{L}
by right exactness of pull-back. Thus we know that
\mathcal{L}\otimes\kappa(p)
has dimension
1
at every point
p\in S
, which implies that
\mathcal{L}
is a line bundle on
P
. Thus we have a short exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{O}_{P}/\mathcal{F}\to\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\mathcal{L}\to 0,

where
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{O}_{P}
is some ideal sheaf.
Since both
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
and
\mathcal{L}
are locally free, they are both flat which implies that
\mathcal{O}_{P}/\mathcal{F}
is also flat, and hence locally free. As a result, we have
\mathcal{F}=0
and hence a short exact sequence

(3.2)

0\to\mathcal{O}_{P}\to\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\mathcal{L}\to 0,

where
\mathcal{L}
is an invertible sheaf on
P
whose restriction to every geometric fiber has degree
-g-1
.

Next, we show that there is a fiberwise
\bm{\mu}_{2}
-action on
X/S
such that
X\to P
is the
\bm{\mu}_{2}
-quotient map. Let
\sqcup_{j}S_{j}\to S
be an étale affine covering such that
(P_{j}:=P\times_{S}S_{j},\mathcal{L}_{j})
is isomorphic to
(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{S_{j}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{S_{j}}}(-g-1))
. Here
\pi_{j}:X_{j}\to S_{j}
,
\varpi_{j}:P_{j}\to S_{j}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{j}
denote the base change of
\pi
,
\varpi
, and
\mathcal{L}
under
S_{j}/S
. Then we get an exact sequence by the base change of (
3.2
):

(3.3)

0\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{j}}\to\varphi_{j,*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{j}}\to\mathcal{L}_{j}\to 0.

Since

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{L}_{j},\mathcal{O}_{P_{j}})\cong H^{1}(P_{j},\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\vee})\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{S_{j}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{S_{j}}}(g+1))=0,

the exact sequence (
3.3
) splits. Thus we have
\varphi_{j,*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{j}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{P_{j}}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{j}
, and the algebra structure is determined by
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\otimes 2}\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{j}}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{j}
, which corresponds to
(h_{2},h_{1})\in H^{0}(P_{j},\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\otimes-2}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\otimes-1})
. Similar to the proof of Theorem
3.3
, under an automorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{P_{j}}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{j}
given by
(t_{1},t_{2})\mapsto(t_{1}-t_{2}\otimes\frac{h_{1}}{2},t_{2})
, we have a new isomorphism
\varphi_{j,*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{j}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{P_{j}}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{j}
where the algebra structure on the latter is given by
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\otimes 2}\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{j}}
corresponding to
h_{2}+\frac{h_{1}^{2}}{4}
. Thus we have an involution
\tau_{j}:X_{j}\to X_{j}
where
\tau_{j}
acts as
\diag(1,-1)
on
\mathcal{O}_{P_{j}}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{j}
.

Next, we show that
\{\tau_{j}\}
satisfies étale descent and hence gives a non-trivial involution
\tau:X\to X
leaving
P
fixed. Indeed, if
T\to S_{j}
is a morphism, and
\tau_{T}:X_{T}\to X_{T}
with
X_{T}:=X_{j}\times_{S_{j}}T
is a non-trivial involution that leaves
P_{T}:=P_{j}\times_{S_{j}}T
fixed, then we claim that
\tau_{T}
is the pull-back of
\tau_{j}
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{+}
and
\mathcal{F}_{-}
be the
\tau_{T}
-eigensheaves of
\varphi_{T,*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{T}}
corresponding to eigenvalues
1
and
-1
. Then we have
\varphi_{T,*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{T}}\cong\mathcal{F}_{+}\oplus\mathcal{F}_{-}
which implies that both
\mathcal{F}_{+}
and
\mathcal{F}_{-}
are locally free of rank
1
as they are non-zero.
Moreover, since
\mathcal{O}_{P_{T}}
is contained in
\mathcal{F}_{+}
, we have a surjection
\mathcal{L}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{-}
which implies that
\mathcal{L}\cong\mathcal{F}_{-}
and hence
\mathcal{O}_{P_{T}}\cong\mathcal{F}_{+}
. Therefore,
\tau_{T}
and the pull-back of
\tau_{j}
have the same eigensheaves which implies that they are the same. This shows that
\{\tau_{j}\}
satisfies étale descent and gives a construction of
\tau
. Thus (
3.2
) splits and we have
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{P}\oplus\mathcal{L}
as the decomposition of
\tau
-eigensheaves corresponding to eigenvalues
1
and
-1
. Therefore, the algebra structure on
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
is determined by
i:\mathcal{L}^{\otimes 2}\to\mathcal{O}_{P}
. As a result, for each family
\pi:X\to S
of hyperelliptic curves we found a triple
(P/S,\mathcal{L},i)\in\mathscr{H}_{g}^{\prime}(S)
such that
(X\to S)
is isomorphic to
F(P/S,\mathcal{L},i)
. Thus
F
is essentially surjective.

Finally, we show that
F
is fully faithful. This is equivalent to showing that for any
(X\to S)\in\mathscr{H}_{g}(S)
, the triple
(P/S,\mathcal{L},i)\in\mathscr{H}_{g}^{\prime}(S)
whose image under
F
is
(X\to S)
is unique up to unique isomorphism.
This can be reduced to showing that the factorization
X\xrightarrow{\varphi}P\xrightarrow{\varpi}S
of
f:X\to S
is unique up to unique isomorphism. Indeed, once this is shown, then
\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{coker}(\mathcal{O}_{P}\to\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X})
, and there is a unique splitting
\mathcal{L}\hookrightarrow\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
such that the multiplication map sends
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes 2}
to
\mathcal{O}_{P}
, which implies that both
\mathcal{L}
and
i
are unique up to unique isomorphism.

Since
\varphi
is finite and Gorenstein, by Grothendieck duality we have

\varphi_{*}\omega_{X/P}\cong(\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\vee}\cong\mathcal{O}_{P}\oplus\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\cong\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\otimes\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}.

Thus we have
\omega_{X/P}\cong\varphi^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\vee}
and hence

\omega_{X/S}\cong\omega_{X/P}\otimes\varphi^{*}\omega_{P/S}\cong\varphi^{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{P/S}).

In particular,
\omega_{X/S}
is
f
-globally generated as
\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{P/S}
restricts to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(g-1)
on every geometric fiber of
\varpi
.
Hence

f_{*}\omega_{X/S}\cong f_{*}\varphi^{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{P/S})\cong\varpi_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{P/S}\otimes\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X})\cong\varpi_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{P/S})\oplus\varpi_{*}\omega_{P/S}.

Since
\omega_{P/S}
restricts to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-2)
on every geometric fiber of
\varpi
, we know that
\varpi_{*}\omega_{P/S}=0
. As a result, we have
f_{*}\omega_{X/S}\cong\varpi_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{P/S})
. Therefore, the relative canonical map
X\to\mathbf{P}:=\Proj_{S}\mathrm{Sym}f_{*}\omega_{X/S}
factors through the closed embedding of
P\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}
induced by the
\varpi
-very ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{P/S}
. Since
\mathcal{O}_{P}
injects into
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
, we know that
X\to P
is isomorphic to the scheme theoretic image map of
X\to\mathbf{P}
up to a unique isomorphism. Thus we have shown that
F
is fully faithful.

To summarize, we have shown that
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{H}_{g}^{\prime}
are equivalent as fibered categories. Thus the proof is finished by (
3.1
).
∎

Theorem 3.15
.

The functor
\mathscr{R}_{g}
is represented by an algebraic stack of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
. Moreover, we have an isomorphism of stacks

\mathscr{R}_{g}\cong[\mathbb{A}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1)))/(\mathrm{GL}(2)/\bm{\mu}_{g+1})],

where
\mathrm{GL}(2)/\bm{\mu}_{g+1}
-action is induced by the standard isomorphism
\mathrm{GL}(2)\cong\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1))
.

Proof.

First of all, we show that
\mathscr{R}_{g}
is a prestack, i.e. a category fibered in groupoids. Suppose
(f:X\to S)\in\mathscr{R}_{g}(S)
. Let
g:S^{\prime}\to S
be a morphism of locally Noetherian
\mathbbm{k}
-schemes. Then we have
f^{\prime}:X^{\prime}=X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
as the base change of
f
under
g
. Clearly,
f^{\prime}
is the pull-back of
f
under
g
in the stack
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
. Thus it suffices to show that
f^{\prime}\in\mathscr{R}_{g}(S^{\prime})
. Let
\varpi^{\prime}:P^{\prime}=P\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
and
\psi^{\prime}:P^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
be the base change of
\varpi
and
\psi
under
g
respectively. Then clearly
P^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration, and
\psi^{\prime}
is a closed immersion such that
f^{\prime}=\psi^{\prime}\circ\varpi^{\prime}
. Denote by
g_{X}:X^{\prime}\to X
the base change of
g
under
f
. Since
P
is flat over
S
, we know that
g_{X}^{*}\mathcal{N}
is the ideal sheaf
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
of
P^{\prime}
in
X^{\prime}
, which implies that
\mathcal{N}^{\prime 2}=0
and
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{P^{\prime}}
-module of rank
1
. Thus
f^{\prime}\in\mathscr{R}_{g}(S^{\prime})
.

Next, we define a functor
\mathscr{R}_{g}^{\prime}
from locally Noetherian
\mathbbm{k}
-schemes to groupoids as

\mathscr{R}_{g}^{\prime}(S):=\left\{(P/S,\mathcal{L},\mathcal{E})\left|\begin{array}[]{l}P/S\textrm{ is a $\mathbb{P}^{1}$-fibration, $\mathcal{L}$ is a line bundle on $P$ whose}\\
\textrm{restriction to every geometric fiber has degree $-g-1$,}\\
\textrm{and $\mathcal{E}$ is an extension of $\Omega_{P/S}$ by $\mathcal{L}$.}\end{array}\right.\right\}.

Here the morphisms of
\mathscr{R}_{g}^{\prime}(S)
are defined in the way that
(P/S,\mathcal{L},\mathcal{E})\to(P^{\prime}/S,\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathcal{E}^{\prime})
consists of an isomorphism
h:P\xrightarrow{\cong}P^{\prime}
of
S
-schemes and an isomorphism between exact sequences
(0\to\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{E}\to\Omega_{P/S}\to 0)
and
(0\to h^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\to h^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\to h^{*}\Omega_{P^{\prime}/S}\to 0)
extending the natural identification
\Omega_{P/S}=h^{*}\Omega_{P^{\prime}/S}
. It is clear that
\mathscr{R}_{g}^{\prime}
is a prestack by flatness of
P/S
.
We claim that
\mathscr{R}_{g}^{\prime}
is a stack and

(3.4)

\mathscr{R}_{g}^{\prime}\cong[\mathbb{A}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1)))/(\mathrm{GL}(2)/\bm{\mu}_{g+1})].

Similar to
[
AV04
, Proof of Theorem 4.1]
, we consider the auxiliary fibered category
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g}^{\prime}
whose objects over
S
are pairs consisting of
(P/S,\mathcal{L},\mathcal{E})\in\mathscr{R}_{g}(S)
plus an isomorphism
p:(P,\mathcal{L})\to(\mathbb{P}_{S}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{S}^{1}}(-g-1))
over
S
. The arrows in
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g}^{\prime}
are arrows in
\mathscr{R}_{g}^{\prime}
preserving
p
. From the standard theory of extensions, we know that
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g}^{\prime}
is indeed equivalent to a category fibered in sets where
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g}^{\prime}(S)
is equivalent to the set
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathbb{P}_{S}^{1}}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}_{S}^{1}/S},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{S}^{1}}(-g-1))
. By Leray’s spectral sequence and flat base change
S\to\Spec(\mathbbm{k})
, one can see that

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathbb{P}_{S}^{1}}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}_{S}^{1}/S},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}_{S}^{1}}(-g-1))\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1))\otimes_{\mathbbm{k}}H^{0}(S,\mathcal{O}_{S}).

Moreover, the above isomorphism commutes with arbitrary base change
S^{\prime}\to S
.
As a result, the functor
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}^{\prime}_{g}
is represented by the
\mathbbm{k}
-scheme
\mathbb{A}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1)))
. Then following similar arguments to
[
AV04
, Proof of Theorem 4.1]
, we have the equivalence (
3.4
) as
\mathrm{GL}(2)/\bm{\mu}_{g+1}
is precisely
\Aut(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1))
.

Next, we show that
\mathscr{R}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}^{\prime}
are equivalent as fibered categories. We construct a functor
F:\mathscr{R}_{g}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{R}_{g}
following
[
BE95
, Proof of Theorem 1.2]
. Given
(P/S,\mathcal{L},\mathcal{E})\in\mathscr{R}_{g}^{\prime}(S)
, let
\mathcal{O}_{X}
be a sheaf of
\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules obtained as the pullback of
\mathcal{O}_{P}\xrightarrow{d}\Omega_{P/S}
under
\mathcal{E}\to\Omega_{P/S}
, so that we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{P/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

We define a
\varpi^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}
-algebra structure on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
in the following way. Let
a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathcal{O}_{P}(U)
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathcal{E}(U)
where
U\subset P
is open, so that
d(a_{i})=\gamma(x_{i})
and hence
(a_{i},x_{i})\in\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
. Then we define

(a_{1},x_{1})(a_{2},x_{2}):=(a_{1}a_{2},a_{1}x_{2}+a_{2}x_{1}).

The product is in
\mathcal{O}_{X}(U)
as
d
is a derivation. Then we define
X:=\Spec\mathcal{O}_{X}
with the underlying topological space
|X|=|P|
.
Following
[
BE95
, Proof of Theorem 1.2]
, it is not hard to check that
X\to S
is a family of ribbons and
\mathcal{E}\cong\Omega_{X/S}|_{P}
.

To show that
F
is an equivalence of fibered categories, it suffices to show that
F
is fully faithful and essentially surjective. For each family of ribbons
X\to S
, by smoothness of
P/S
we have a short exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{N}\to\Omega_{X/P}|_{P}\to\Omega_{P/S}\to 0.

Thus following
[
BE95
, Proof of Theorem 1.2]
it is not hard to check that
(X\to S)
is isomorphic to
F(P/S,\mathcal{N},\Omega_{X/P}|_{P})
. This shows that
F
is essentially surjective.

Finally, we show that
F
is fully faithful. Let
(X\to S)\in\mathscr{R}_{g}(S)
be a family of ribbons, which is isomorphic to an image of
F(S)
by essential surjectivity of
F
. Since both
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{L}
are uniquely determined (up to unique isomorphism) by the ideal sheaf
\mathcal{N}
of
P
in
X
, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{N}
is unique. This is clear if
S
is reduced: notice that
P
is reduced as
P\to S
is flat with reduced fibers. Thus the ideal
\mathcal{N}
contains the nilradical sheaf
\mathcal{N}_{X}
of
X
, while
\mathcal{N}^{2}=0
implies that
\mathcal{N}
is contained in
\mathcal{N}_{X}
, and hence
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{X}
is unique.

It remains to show that
\mathcal{N}
is unique for a general base scheme
S
.
Assume that we have two ideal sheaves
\mathcal{N}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
defining two
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibrations
P/S
and
P^{\prime}/S
respectively. Our goal is to show
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
. By étale descent, we may replace
S
by an affine étale cover such that
P
is a trivial
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibrations. In other words, we may assume that
S=\Spec R
and
P=\mathbb{P}_{R}^{1}
. Let
x\in X
be a scheme-theoretic point. It suffices to show the localizations
\mathcal{N}_{x}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{x}
are the same. Let
(B,\mathfrak{m}):=(\mathcal{O}_{P,x},\mathfrak{m}_{P,x})
, then
R\to B
is flat. Since any extension
\mathcal{E}
of
\mathcal{N}
and
\Omega_{P/S}
splits locally at
x
, we may identify
\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
with
B\otimes_{k}k[t]/(t^{2})=B[t]/(t^{2})
, under which
\mathcal{N}_{x}=(t)
. Since
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{P}^{\prime}
-module of rank
1
, it is locally a principal ideal. Thus there exist
b_{0},b_{1}\in B
such that
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{x}=(b_{0}+b_{1}t)
. Since
B
is a local ring of
P=\mathbb{P}_{R}^{1}
, we have
B_{\mathrm{red}}\cong B\otimes_{R}R_{\mathrm{red}}
. Moreover, from the previous discussion on the case of reduced
S
we have that
\phi_{B}(\mathcal{N}_{x})=\phi_{B}(\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{x})
has the same image under the quotient ring map
\phi_{B}:B[t]/(t^{2})\to B_{\mathrm{red}}[t]/(t^{2})
. Let
N_{B}
be the nilradical of
B
. Then there exists
c_{0}+c_{1}t\in B[t]/(t^{2})
such that
\phi_{B}(c_{0}+c_{1}t)
is invertible and

b_{0}+b_{1}t-t(c_{0}+c_{1}t)\in N_{B}[t]/(t^{2}).

In particular, we have
b_{0}\in N_{B}
and
b_{1}-c_{0}\in N_{B}
.
The invertibility of
\phi_{B}(c_{0}+c_{1}t)
is equivalent to
c_{0}\in B^{\times}=B\setminus\mathfrak{m}
. Since
N_{B}\subset\mathfrak{m}
, we know that
b_{1}\not\in\mathfrak{m}
which implies
b_{1}\in B^{\times}
. Recall that
\mathcal{N}^{\prime 2}=0
from definition, and hence
(b_{0}+b_{1}t)^{2}=0
in
B[t]/(t^{2})
which implies that
b_{0}b_{1}=0
. Since
b_{1}
is invertible, we have
b_{0}=0
and hence
\mathcal{N}_{x}^{\prime}=(b_{1}t)=(t)=\mathcal{N}_{x}
. Thus we have shown that
F
is fully faithful.

To summarize, we have shown that
\mathscr{R}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}^{\prime}
are equivalent as fibered categories. Thus the proof is finished by (
3.4
).
∎

Proposition 3.16
.

The hyperelliptic ribbon
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
is the only closed point in
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}
. The automorphism group
\Aut(R_{\mathrm{hyp}})
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{GL}(2)/\bm{\mu}_{g+1}
. Moreover,
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
is contained in
\overline{\{x\}}
for any point
x
in
\mathscr{H}_{g}
or
\mathscr{R}_{g}
.

Proof.

From the construction it is straightforward to see that
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
corresponds to the origin in both quotient stack presentations of
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}
. Thus the claim is clear by examining the
\mathrm{GL}(2)/\bm{\mu}_{g+1}
-action on both affine spaces, where the last statement follows from the fact that the
\mathbb{G}_{m}(\cong\{\diag(t,t)\mid t\in\mathbbm{k}^{\times}\}/\bm{\mu}_{g+1})
-action on both affine spaces is a scaling.
∎

3.4.
Deformation theory of the hyperelliptic ribbon

Theorem 3.17
.

The deformation of the hyperelliptic ribbon of genus
g\geq 2
is unobstructed.

Proof.

Let
C
be the hyperelliptic ribbon of genus
g\geq 2
, i.e.
C=(z^{2}=0)\subset\mathbb{P}(1,1,g+1)
.

Denote by
S=\mathbb{P}(1,1,g+1)\setminus\{[0,0,1]\}
. We have the closed immersion
\psi:\mathbb{P}^{1}\hookrightarrow C
and the projection maps
f:S\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\varphi=f|_{C}:C\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We can treat
S
as the total space of
L^{\vee}
over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
where
L=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1)
. Denote by
\mathcal{I}
the ideal sheaf of
C
in
S
.

From deformation theory we know that the tangent space (resp. the obstruction space) of
\mathrm{Def}_{C}
is given by
\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{1}(\mathbf{L}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})
(resp.
\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{2}(\mathbf{L}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})
), where
\mathbf{L}_{C}
denotes the cotangent complex of
C
over
\mathbbm{k}
. Since
C
is a local complete intersection in
S
, we know that
\mathbf{L}_{C}
is quasi-isomorphic to the mapping cone
[\mathcal{I}/\mathcal{I}^{2}\to\Omega_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}]
by
[
Sta25
,
Tag 08SL
]
. Thus we have the following long exact sequence

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Hom}_{C}(\mathbf{L}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{C}(\Omega_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{C}(\mathcal{I}/\mathcal{I}^{2},\mathcal{O}_{C})

(3.5)

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{1}(\mathbf{L}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})\to\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{1}(\Omega_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})\to\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{1}(\mathcal{I}/\mathcal{I}^{2},\mathcal{O}_{C})

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{2}(\mathbf{L}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})\to\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{2}(\Omega_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C}).

To show unobstructedness of
\mathrm{Def}_{C}
, it suffices to show that
\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{2}(\mathbf{L}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})=0
. First of all, we have

\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{i}(\Omega_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})\cong H^{i}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}),

which implies
\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{2}(\Omega_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})=0

by the Grothendieck vanishing theorem. Next, notice that
\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{O}_{S}(-2C_{\mathrm{red}})\cong f^{*}L^{\otimes 2}
and thus we have
\mathcal{I}/\mathcal{I}^{2}\cong\varphi^{*}L^{\otimes 2}
. This implies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{i}(\mathcal{I}/\mathcal{I}^{2},\mathcal{O}_{C})

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{i}(\varphi^{*}L^{\otimes 2},\mathcal{O}_{C})\cong H^{i}(C,\varphi^{*}L^{\otimes-2})

\displaystyle\cong H^{i}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\otimes-2}\otimes\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C})\cong H^{i}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\otimes-2}\oplus L^{\vee}).

Here we use
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\oplus L
in the last isomorphism. Thus we have

\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{1}(\mathcal{I}/\mathcal{I}^{2},\mathcal{O}_{C})\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\otimes-2}\oplus L^{\vee})=H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(2g+2)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(g+1))=0.

Therefore, we have
\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{2}(\mathbf{L}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})=0
by (
3.5
). The proof is finished.
∎

Corollary 3.18
.

The deformation of a hyperelliptic curve or a ribbon of genus
g\geq 2
is unobstructed.

Proof.

Let
C
be a hyperelliptic curve or a ribbon. Then by Proposition
3.16
we know that
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
is contained in the closure of
[C]
in
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
. By Theorem
3.17
we know that
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
is smooth at
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
. Thus
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
is smooth at
[C]
as well, i.e. the deformation of
C
is unobstructed.
∎

Proposition 3.19
.

Let
C=(z^{2}=0)\subset\mathbb{P}(1,1,g+1)
be the hyperelliptic ribbon of genus
g\geq 2
. Let
S
be the smooth locus of
\mathbb{P}(1,1,g+1)
. Recall that
\mathrm{Def}_{C}
represents the functor of deformations of
C
. Denote by
\mathrm{Def}_{C\hookrightarrow S}
(resp.
\mathrm{Def}^{\prime}_{C}
) the functor of embedded deformations of
C
in
S
(resp. locally trivial deformations of
C
).
Then the tangent space of
\mathrm{Def}_{C}
is a direct sum of the image of the tangent space of
\mathrm{Def}_{C\hookrightarrow S}
and the tangent space of
\mathrm{Def}_{C}^{\prime}
. Moreover, this can be written as

\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{1}(\mathbf{L}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})\cong H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\otimes-2})\oplus\mathrm{Ext}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},L),

where
L=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1)
.

Proof.

Denote by
\mathcal{N}_{C/S}:=(\mathcal{I}/\mathcal{I}^{2})^{\vee}
the normal sheaf of
C
in
S
.
From deformation theory, we know that the tangent space of
\mathrm{Def}_{C}
(resp. of
\mathrm{Def}^{\prime}_{C}
, of
\mathrm{Def}_{C\hookrightarrow S}
) is given by
\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{1}(\mathbf{L}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})
(resp.
H^{1}(C,T_{C})
,
H^{0}(C,\mathcal{N}_{C/S})
). Recall from (
3.5
) the long exact sequence

H^{0}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})\xrightarrow{\beta}H^{0}(C,\mathcal{N}_{C/S})\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathrm{Ext}_{C}^{1}(\mathbf{L}_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C})\to H^{1}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})\to 0.

Thus it suffices to show the following two statements.

(1)

\mathrm{im}(\gamma)\cong H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\otimes-2})
;

(2)

H^{1}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})\cong H^{1}(C,T_{C})\cong\mathrm{Ext}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},L)
.

We have the normal exact sequence

0\to T_{C}\to T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}\to\mathcal{N}_{C/S}.

From the proof of Theorem
3.17
we know that
\mathcal{N}_{C/S}\cong\varphi^{*}L^{\otimes-2}
is a line bundle on
C
.
By local computation, we know that the image of
T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}\to\mathcal{N}_{C/S}
is precisely
\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S}
where
\mathcal{N}
is the nilradical sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
. Thus we have a short exact sequence

0\to T_{C}\to T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}\to\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S}\to 0,

which gives a long exact sequence

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\to H^{0}(C,T_{C})\to H^{0}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})\xrightarrow{\beta^{\prime}}H^{0}(C,\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S})

\displaystyle\to H^{1}(C,T_{C})\to H^{1}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})\to H^{1}(C,\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S}).

We first show that
H^{1}(C,\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S})=0
. We have

\varphi_{*}(\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S})\cong\varphi_{*}(\mathcal{N}\cdot\varphi^{*}L^{\otimes-2})\cong(\varphi_{*}\mathcal{N})\otimes L^{\otimes-2}\cong L\otimes L^{\otimes-2}\cong L^{\vee}.

Thus

(3.6)

H^{i}(C,\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S})\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\varphi_{*}(\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S}))\cong H^{i}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\vee})\cong H^{i}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(g+1)).

As a result, we have
H^{1}(C,\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S})\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(g+1))=0
.

Next, we show that
\beta^{\prime}
is surjective. From (
3.6
) we have

h^{0}(C,\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S})=h^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(g+1))=g+2.

By Proposition
3.16
we have
h^{0}(C,T_{C})=\dim\Aut(C)=4
. It is clear that
T_{S}
fits into an exact sequence

0\to f^{*}L^{\vee}\to T_{S}\to f^{*}T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\to 0,

which implies the following exact sequence

0\to\varphi^{*}L^{\vee}\to T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}\to\varphi^{*}T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\to 0.

Thus we have an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\to H^{0}(C,\varphi^{*}L^{\vee})\to H^{0}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})\to H^{0}(C,\varphi^{*}T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}})

(3.7)

\displaystyle\to H^{1}(C,\varphi^{*}L^{\vee})\to H^{1}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})\to H^{1}(C,\varphi^{*}T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}})\to H^{2}(C,\varphi^{*}L^{\vee})=0

We have
H^{1}(C,\varphi^{*}L^{\vee})\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\vee}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}})=0
. Thus we can determine the dimension as

\displaystyle h^{0}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})

\displaystyle=h^{0}(C,\varphi^{*}L^{\vee})+h^{0}(C,\varphi^{*}T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}})

\displaystyle=h^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\vee}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}})+h^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\oplus T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\otimes L)=g+6.

Thus we have
h^{0}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})=h^{0}(C,T_{C})+h^{0}(C,\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S})
, which implies that
\beta^{\prime}
is surjective.

Combining
H^{1}(C,\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S})=0
and surjectivity of
\beta^{\prime}
, we conclude that
H^{1}(C,T_{C})\cong H^{1}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})
and that
\mathrm{im}(\beta)=H^{0}(C,\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S})\subset H^{0}(C,\mathcal{N}_{C/S})
. We have

\varphi_{*}\mathcal{N}_{C/S}\cong(\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C})\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\otimes-2}\cong L^{\vee}\oplus L^{\otimes-2}.

Under this isomorphism,
\varphi_{*}(\mathcal{N}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{C/S})
is mapped to
L^{\vee}
. Thus we have

\mathrm{im}(\gamma)=\mathrm{coker}(\beta)\cong H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\vee}\oplus L^{\otimes-2})/H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\vee})\cong H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\otimes-2}).

This proves (1).

Finally we prove (2). By (
3.7
) and the fact that
H^{1}(C,\varphi^{*}L^{\vee})=0
, we have

\displaystyle H^{1}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})

\displaystyle\cong H^{1}(C,\varphi^{*}T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}})\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1},(\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C})\otimes T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}})

\displaystyle\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1},T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\oplus T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\otimes L)\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{1},T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\otimes L)\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},L).

This together with
H^{1}(C,T_{C})\cong H^{1}(C,T_{S}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})
proves (2).
∎

4.
Moduli of curves via canonical maps

In this section, we construct a new stack
\mathscr{X}_{g}
parameterizing Gorenstein smoothable curves
C
of genus
g\geq 3
with ample and basepoint free canonical bundle such that its cycle-theoretic image under the canonical map is Chow semistable (see Definition
4.2
).
The purpose of introducing
\mathscr{X}_{g}
is to (potentially) provide a wall crossing replacing hyperelliptic curves by ribbons, which is verified in Section
5
for
g=4
. The main result of this section (Theorem
4.12
) shows that there exists an open neighborhood
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
of
\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}
in
\mathscr{X}_{g}
that admits a good moduli space isomorphic to an open subscheme of the Chow quotient space
\mathfrak{X}_{g}^{\rm c}
.

4.1.
Stack of Gorenstein curves with Chow semistable cycle

Definition-Proposition 4.1
.

There exists an open substack
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
that parameterizes curves
C\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
whose canonical bundle
\omega_{C}
is ample and basepoint free. Moreover,
\mathcal{M}_{g}
is an open substack of
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
is an algebraic stack locally of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
, it suffices to show that for every scheme
U
of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
together with a smooth morphism
U\to\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
, the locus

V:=\{u\in U\mid\omega_{C_{u}}\textrm{ is ample and basepoint free}\}

is open in
U
. Here
f:C_{U}\to U
denotes the pull-back of the universal curve over
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
, and
C_{u}:=C_{U}\times_{U}k(u)
. Since
f
is Gorenstein with equidimensional fibers of dimension
1
, we know that
\omega_{C_{U}/U}
is an invertible sheaf that commutes with base change. Moreover,
f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{U}
and
R^{1}f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C_{U}}
is a locally free sheaf of rank
g
. By Grothendieck duality,
R^{1}f_{*}\omega_{C_{U}/U}\cong\mathcal{O}_{U}
and the sheaf
f_{*}\omega_{C_{U}/U}\cong(R^{1}f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C_{U}})^{*}
is also locally free of rank
g
.

Clearly, the ampleness of
\omega_{C_{u}}
is an open condition; denote the locus of
u
for which
\omega_{C_{u}}
is ample by
U^{\prime}\subset U
. The restriction
f^{\prime}:C_{U^{\prime}}=f^{-1}(U^{\prime})\to U^{\prime}
of
f
is a projective morphism of schemes of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
. By cohomology and base change, we know that
f^{\prime}_{*}\omega_{C_{U^{\prime}}/U^{\prime}}
commutes with base change. Thus for
u\in U^{\prime}
,
\omega_{C_{u}}
is basepoint free if and only if
\psi_{f^{\prime}}:f^{\prime*}f^{\prime}_{*}\omega_{C_{U^{\prime}}/U^{\prime}}\to\omega_{C_{U^{\prime}}/{U^{\prime}}}
is surjective along
C_{u}
. If
u\in V
, then
\psi_{f^{\prime}}
being surjective on
C_{u}
implies that it is surjective in a neighborhood of
C_{u}
as the surjectivity of a morphism between locally free sheaves is an open condition. Thus
V
contains an open neighborhood of
u
in
U^{\prime}
by properness of
f^{\prime}
, which implies that
V
is open in
U
. This shows that
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
is an open substack of
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
. Moreover, since every smooth curve
C
of genus
g\geq 2
satisfies that
\omega_{C}
is basepoint free, we know that
\mathcal{M}_{g}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
is an open substack.
∎

Definition 4.2
.

Let
\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{\!g}
be the stack-theoretic closure of
\mathcal{M}_{g}
in
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
. Since
\mathcal{M}_{g}
is a smooth irreducible open substack in
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
, we know that
\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{\!g}
is an irreducible component of
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
with reduced stack structure.

Recall from Section
2.4
that
\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}
is the seminormalization of the irreducible component of the Chow scheme
\bm{C}_{1,2g-2}(\mathbb{P}^{g-1})
containing smooth canonical genus
g
curves. Denote by
\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{\!g}^{\rm sn}
the seminormalization of
\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{\!g}
(see e.g.
[
ABB+23
, Definition 13.6]
for the definition of seminormalization of algebraic stacks).
Then we have the Hilbert–Chow morphism
\overline{\Phi}_{g}^{\rm sn}:\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{\!g}^{\rm sn}\to[\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]
. There is an open substack

(\overline{\Phi}_{g}^{\rm sn})^{-1}(\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c})\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{\!g}^{\rm sn}
,
where
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c}=[\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}^{\rm ss}/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]
as in Definition
2.15
.

Let
\mathscr{X}_{g}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{\!g}
be an open substack with seminormalization
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm sn}=(\overline{\Phi}_{g}^{\rm sn})^{-1}(\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c})
. Since seminormalization is a universal homeomorphism, such an open substack
\mathscr{X}_{g}
exists. In particular,
\mathscr{X}_{g}
parameterizes smoothable curves
C\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
such that
\omega_{C}
is ample and basepoint free, and that the Hilbert–Chow image of
C
induced by
|\omega_{C}|
is a Chow-semistable
1
-cycle in
\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
. Denote by
\Phi_{g}^{\rm sn}:\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm sn}\to\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c}
the restriction of
\overline{\Phi}_{g}^{\rm sn}
.

Theorem 4.3
.

Let
g\geq 3
be an integer. Then
\mathscr{X}_{g}
is an irreducible algebraic stack of finite type with affine diagonal that contains
\mathcal{M}_{g}
as a dense open substack. Moreover,
\mathscr{X}_{g}
contains a smooth open neighborhood of the point
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
in
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
.

To prove this theorem, we first show a more general boundedness result.

Lemma 4.4
.

For any
g\geq 2
, the algebraic stack
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
is of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
is locally of finite type, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
is quasi-compact, or equivalently, the curves
C\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}(\mathbbm{k})
are bounded. Let
\varphi:C\to\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
be the finite morphism induced by the basepoint free linear system
|\omega_{C}|
. We first show that
\omega_{C}^{\otimes 3}
is very ample. Since
H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{g-1},\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C}(2))\cong H^{1}(C,\omega_{C}^{\otimes 2})\cong H^{0}(C,\omega_{C}^{\vee})^{\vee}=0
, we know that
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C}
is
3
-regular in the sense of Castelnuovo–Mumford regularity. By
[
Laz04
, Theorem 1.8.3]
, we know that

H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{g-1},\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C}(3))\otimes H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{g-1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{g-1}}(m))\to H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{g-1},\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C}(3+m))

is surjective for every
m\geq 0
. As a result, we know that

H^{0}(C,\omega_{C}^{\otimes 3})\otimes H^{0}(C,\omega_{C}^{\otimes m})\to H^{0}(C,\omega_{C}^{\otimes(3+m)})

is surjective for every
m\geq 0
, which implies that
\omega_{C}^{\otimes 3}
is very ample. By the Riemann–Roch formula for Gorenstein curves
[
Sta25
,
Tag 0BS6
]
, we know that

h^{0}(C,\omega_{C}^{\otimes 3m})=\chi(C,\omega_{C}^{\otimes 3m})=3m(2g-2)-g+1.

Thus the Hilbert polynomial of
(C,\omega_{C}^{\otimes 3})
is independent of the choice of
C
. This together with very ampleness of
\omega_{C}^{\otimes 3}
implies the boundedness of
C\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}(\mathbbm{k})
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.3
.

First we show that
\mathscr{X}_{g}
is irreducible and contains a dense open substack
\mathcal{M}_{g}
. By Lemma
2.16
we know that the
1
-cycle of a smooth canonical curve of genus
g
is Chow stable. The
1
-cycle as the Hilbert–Chow image of a smooth hyperelliptic curve of genus
g
is the doubled rational normal curve in
\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
, which is Chow polystable by Lemma
2.16
. Thus we have
\mathcal{M}_{g}\subset\mathscr{X}_{g}
.
Since
\mathcal{M}_{g}
is open and dense in
\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{\!g}
, it is also open and dense in
\mathscr{X}_{g}
which implies the irreducibility of
\mathscr{X}_{g}
.

Next, since
\mathscr{X}_{g}
is a locally closed substack of
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
, by Lemma
4.4
we know that
\mathscr{X}_{g}
is of finite type. In addition, since every curve
C
in
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
satisfies that
\omega_{C}
is ample, we conclude that
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
and hence
\mathscr{X}_{g}
has affine diagonal.

Finally, we show that
\mathscr{X}_{g}
contains a smooth open neighborhood of
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
in
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
. By Theorem
3.17
we know that the deformations of
R_{\rm hyp}
are unobstructed, which implies that
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
is smooth and hence irreducible in a neighborhood of
R_{\rm hyp}
. Clearly,
\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{\!g}
is an irreducible component of
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
. Since
R_{\mathrm{hyp}}
is smoothable as it is a degeneration of smooth hyperelliptic curves, the irreducibility of
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
at
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
implies that
\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{\!g}
contains a smooth open neighborhood of
R_{\mathrm{hyp}}
in
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
. Moreover, by openness of Chow-semistability we know that
\mathscr{X}_{g}
also contains a smooth open neighborhood of
R_{\mathrm{hyp}}
in
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
.
∎

4.2.
Closed substacks parameterizing hyperelliptic curves and ribbons

Proposition 4.5
.

Both
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}
are closed substacks of
\mathscr{X}_{g}
.

Proof.

From Definition
3.12
, we know that both
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}
admit monomorphisms to
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
. Since
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
is contained in the closure of every point in
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}
by Proposition
3.16
, we know that the images of
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}
in
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
are contained in every open neighborhood of
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
. Thus by Theorem
4.3
we have monomorphisms
\iota_{\mathrm{hyp}}:\mathscr{H}_{g}\to\mathscr{X}_{g}
and
\iota_{\rib}:\mathscr{R}_{g}\to\mathscr{X}_{g}
. Since all three stacks
\mathscr{H}_{g}
,
\mathscr{R}_{g}
and
\mathscr{X}_{g}
are of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
, we know that both
\iota_{\mathrm{hyp}}
and
\iota_{\rib}
are quasi-finite. Since monomorphisms of algebraic spaces are separated
[
Sta25
,
Tag 042N
]
, we know that both
\iota_{\mathrm{hyp}}
and
\iota_{\rib}
are representable by schemes by
[
Sta25
,
Tag 0418
]
. Therefore, to show
\iota_{\mathrm{hyp}}
and
\iota_{\rib}
to be closed immersions, it suffices to show their properness by
[
Sta25
,
Tag 04XV
]
, which further reduces to checking the existence part of the valuative criterion for properness given their separatedness.

Let
f:X\to\Spec\,R
be an object in
\mathscr{X}_{g}(R)
where
R
is a DVR with generic point
\eta
and closed point
\kappa
. By the semicontinuity theorem, we know that
f_{*}\omega_{X/R}
is locally free of rank
g
. Moreover, from the definition of
\mathscr{X}_{g}
we know that
\omega_{X/R}
is
f
-ample and
f
-globally generated. Thus the relative canonical map gives a finite morphism
\varphi:X\to\mathbf{P}:=\Proj_{R}\mathrm{Sym}f_{*}\omega_{X/R}
.

Firstly, we assume that
X_{\eta}\in\mathscr{H}_{g}(\eta)
and aim to show
f\in\mathscr{H}_{g}(R)
.
Denote by
P
the scheme theoretic image of
\varphi
. Then we have an injection
\mathcal{O}_{P}\hookrightarrow\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Since
X
is flat over
R
, we know that
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
is a torsion free
R
-module, which implies that
\mathcal{O}_{P}
is a torsion free
R
-module. Hence
P
is also flat over
R
.
Since
X_{\bar{\eta}}
is a hyperelliptic curve, we know that
P_{\bar{\eta}}
is a rational normal curve in
\mathbf{P}_{\bar{\eta}}
. Hence the
1
-cycle
\varphi_{*}[X_{\bar{\eta}}]
is Chow polystable by Lemma
2.16
. This implies that the
1
-cycle
\varphi_{*}[X_{\bar{\kappa}}]
is also a doubled rational normal curve, which implies that
P_{\bar{\kappa}}
is also a rational normal curve by flatness of
P/R
. As a result,
P\to R
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration. Moreover, we have
\varphi_{\bar{\kappa},*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\bar{\kappa}}}
has rank
2
, which implies that
\varphi_{\bar{\kappa},*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\bar{\kappa}}}
is locally free of rank
2
by Theorem
3.3
. Since
\varphi
is affine, we know that
\varphi_{\bar{\kappa},*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\bar{\kappa}}}
is the pull-back of
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
to the geometric fiber
P_{\bar{\kappa}}
by
[
Sta25
,
Tag 02KG
]
. Thus
\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
has constant rank
2
and hence is locally free, which implies that
f\in\mathscr{H}_{g}(R)
.

Next, we assume that
X_{\eta}\in\mathscr{R}_{g}(\eta)
and aim to show
f\in\mathscr{R}_{g}(R)
. Since
X
is flat over
R
with
X_{\eta}
irreducible, we know that
X
is also irreducible. Let
P:=X_{\mathrm{red}}
with the natural closed immersion
\psi:P\hookrightarrow X
. Since
P
is integral, we know that
P
is flat over
R
. From the previous discussion, we know that
\varphi\circ\psi:P\to\mathbf{P}
is a finite morphism that maps the geometric general fiber
P_{\bar{\eta}}
isomorphically to a rational normal curve in
\mathbf{P}_{\bar{\eta}}
, and the image of
P_{\bar{\kappa}}
is supported on a rational normal curve in
\mathbf{P}_{\bar{\kappa}}
. By flatness of
(\varphi\circ\psi)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P}
over
R
, we conclude that
\varphi\circ\psi
is a closed immersion which makes
P\to\Spec\,R
a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration. Let
\mathcal{N}
be the nilradical sheaf
X
. Then we have
\mathcal{N}_{\eta}^{2}=0
and
\mathcal{N}_{\eta}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{P_{\eta}}
-module of rank
1
by assumption. Thus
\mathcal{N}^{2}
is supported over
\kappa
which implies
\mathcal{N}^{2}=0
by flatness of
X/R
. Moreover, the flatness of
X
and
P
over
R
implies the flatness of
\mathcal{N}
over
R
. Thus
\mathcal{N}
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{P}
-module of rank
1
as
P/R
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration, which implies that
f\in\mathscr{R}_{g}(R)
. The proof is finished.
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

Th point
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
is the only point in the intersection
\mathscr{H}_{g}\cap\mathscr{R}_{g}
and the only closed point in
\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}
. Moreover, the stack
\mathscr{X}_{g}
is smooth in a neighborhood of
\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}
, and

T_{\mathscr{X}_{g},[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]}=T_{\mathscr{H}_{g},[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]}\oplus T_{\mathscr{R}_{g},[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]}.

Proof.

Notice that the canonical map of a hyperelliptic curve is never a closed embedding by Proposition
3.4
, while the canonical map of a non-hyperelliptic ribbon is always a closed embedding by Proposition
3.10
. Thus
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
is the only point in
\mathscr{H}_{g}\cap\mathscr{R}_{g}
. It is the only closed point of
\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}
by Proposition
3.16
. The last statement follows from Corollary
3.18
and Proposition
3.19
.
∎

4.3.
Normality of the Chow scheme

In this subsection, we show that
\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}
is normal in an open neighborhood of the locus parameterizing doubled rational normal curves.

Let
\bm{H}_{\chi_{g}}(\mathbb{P}^{g-1})
be the Hilbert scheme of
\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
for the Hilbert polynomial
\chi_{g}(t):=(2g-2)t-g+1
. Let
\Hilb_{g,1}
be the closure of the locus parameterizing canonical curves of genus
g
in
\bm{H}_{\chi_{g}}(\mathbb{P}^{g-1})
with reduced scheme structure. Then we have a Hilbert–Chow morphism
\Phi_{g,1}:\Hilb_{g,1}^{\rm sn}\to\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}
, where
\nu_{g,1}:\Hilb_{g,1}^{\rm sn}\to\Hilb_{g,1}
denotes the seminormalization. From the construction, we know that
\Phi_{g,1}
is proper birational.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
[C]\in\bm{H}_{\chi_{g}}(\mathbb{P}^{g-1})
be a closed subscheme of
\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
whose corresponding
1
-cycle is a doubled rational normal curve. Then
C
is a canonical ribbon of genus
g
.

Proof.

Let
C\subset\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
be a non-reduced curve with associated Chow cycle
2[C_{0}]
where
C_{0}\subset\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
is a rational normal curve. Let
\mathcal{J}
be the largest finite length ideal sheaf on
C
and define
C_{\rib}
as the corresponding closed subscheme of
C
, i.e.
\mathcal{O}_{C_{\rib}}=\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathcal{J}
. By construction,
C_{\rib}
is
S_{1}
and
(C_{\rib})_{\mathrm{red}}=C_{\mathrm{red}}=C_{0}
and the generic points of
C_{\rib}
and
C
agree. We first show that
C_{\rib}
is a ribbon. Let
\mathcal{N}_{\rib}
be the ideal of nilpotents of
\mathcal{O}_{C_{\rib}}
. As
\mathcal{O}_{C_{\rib}}
has length
2
at its generic point and
\mathcal{O}_{C_{\rib}}
has no finite length ideal sheaves, we conclude
\mathcal{N}_{\rib}^{2}=0
. Thus
\mathcal{N}_{\rib}
has the structure of an
\mathcal{O}_{C_{0}}
-module. Since
\mathcal{O}_{C_{\rib}}
has no nonzero finite length ideal sheaves, we know that
\mathcal{N}_{\rib}
is a torsion free
\mathcal{O}_{C_{0}}
-module of rank
1
.
Therefore, by smoothness of
C_{0}
we conclude that
\mathcal{N}_{\rib}
is a rank 1 locally free
\mathcal{O}_{C_{0}}
-module, and
C_{\rib}
is indeed a ribbon.

From the exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{J}\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\to\mathcal{O}_{C_{\rib}}\to 0

and additivity of Euler characteristics, we conclude

\chi(\mathcal{O}_{C_{\rib}})=\chi(\mathcal{O}_{C})-\chi(\mathcal{J})

and as
\mathcal{J}
is supported on a finite set, this implies

g(C_{\rib})\geq g(C)=g

with equality if and only if
\mathcal{J}=0
and
C_{\rib}=C
. Let
g_{\rib}=g(C_{\rib})
so
C_{\rib}
is a ribbon with conormal bundle
L=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g_{\rib}-1)
. From the exact sequence

0\to L\to\mathcal{O}_{C_{\rib}}\to\mathcal{O}_{C_{0}}\to 0

there is an induced exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{I}_{C_{\rib}}\to\mathcal{I}_{C_{0}}\to L\to 0

where
\mathcal{I}_{C_{\rib}}
,
\mathcal{I}_{C_{0}}
denote the ideal sheaves of
C_{\rib}
,
C_{0}
in
\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
. By
[
BE95
, Lemma 5.4]
,
\mathcal{I}_{C_{0}}/\mathcal{I}_{C_{0}}^{2}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1)^{\oplus(g-2)}
which gives a surjection

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1)^{\oplus(g-2)}\to L\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g_{\rib}-1)\to 0.

This implies
-g_{\rib}-1\geq-g-1
, i.e.
g\geq g_{\rib}
. Combining this with the previous inequality
g_{\rib}\geq g
, we conclude
g_{\rib}=g
and hence
\mathcal{J}=0
and
C_{\rib}=C
. Therefore, if
C
has image
2[C_{0}]
in the Hilbert–Chow morphism, then
C
is a canonical ribbon.
∎

Proposition 4.8
.

There exists a smooth
\mathrm{PGL}(g)
-equivariant open subscheme
\widetilde{U}_{g,1}
of
\Hilb_{g,1}
such that the following hold.

(1)

\widetilde{U}_{g,1}
contains the locus
H_{\rm rib}
parameterizing canonical ribbons.

(2)

The image
U_{g,1}:=\Phi_{g,1}(\widetilde{U}_{g,1})
is an open subscheme of
\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}^{\rm ss}
. Moreover,
U_{g,1}
contains
Z_{\rib}
which is the reduced locally closed subscheme of
\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}
parameterizing doubled rational normal curves.

(3)

Every subscheme
[C\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{g-1}]\in\widetilde{U}_{g,1}
that is not a canonical ribbon satisfies that
[C]\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\rm lci+}
,
\omega_{C}
is very ample and induces the closed embedding
C\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
. Conversely, every such curve
C
whose canonical image gives a Chow semistable
1
-cycle belongs to
\widetilde{U}_{g,1}
.
In particular,
U_{g,1}\setminus Z_{\rm rib}
only contains reduced
1
-cycles.

(4)

The Hilbert–Chow morphism
\widetilde{U}_{g,1}\to U_{g,1}
is proper and surjective, and induces an isomorphism between
\widetilde{U}_{g,1}\setminus H_{\rib}
and
U_{g,1}\setminus Z_{\rib}
. Moreover,
U_{g,1}
is normal.

Proof.

We construct the schemes
U_{g,1}
and
\widetilde{U}_{g,1}
in several steps. Since all constructions are
\mathrm{PGL}(g)
-equivariant, we will omit this adjective in the entire proof whenever we talk about open subschemes in
\Hilb_{g,1}
or
\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}
.

We first show that there is an open neighborhood
U_{1}
of
H_{\rib}
in
\Hilb_{g,1}
that parameterizes curves
C
with lci singularities and
\omega_{C}
is ample and base point free.
From previous discussions we know that the Hilbert–Chow morphism
\Phi_{g,1}:\Hilb_{g,1}^{\rm sn}\to\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}
is a projective birational morphism between projective seminormal varieties. Moreover,
\Phi_{g,1}^{-1}(2[C_{0}])
only consists of canonical ribbons by Lemma
4.7
.
Let
f:\mathcal{C}_{g,1}\to\Hilb_{g,1}
be the universal family. Since
f
is flat and proper of relative dimension
\leq 1
, it induces a morphism of algebraic stacks
\xi:[\Hilb_{g,1}/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]\to\mathcal{C}urves
. From Section
2.1
and Definition-Proposition
4.1
, we know that
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}\cap\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}}
is an open substack of
\mathcal{C}urves
. Since canonical ribbons of genus
g
belong to
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}\cap\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}}
, there exists an open neighborhood
U_{1}
of
H_{\rib}
in
\Hilb_{g,1}
such that
[U_{1}/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]=\xi^{-1}(\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}\cap\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}})
.

Next, we show that there exists an open neighborhood
U_{3}
of
H_{\rib}
in
U_{1}
that precisely parameterizes curves
[C]\in U_{1}
such that
\omega_{C}
is very ample and gives the embedding
C\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
.
Since curves in
H_{\rib}
are non-degenerate, by openness of non-degeneracy there exists an open neighborhood
U_{2}
of
H_{\rib}
in
U_{1}
that precisely parameterizes non-degenerate curves in
U_{1}
.
Denote by
f_{2}:\mathcal{C}_{U_{2}}\to U_{2}
the base change of
f
. Let us consider the invertible sheaf
\mathcal{F}:=\omega_{\mathcal{C}_{U_{2}}/U_{2}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{U_{2}}}(-1)
on
\mathcal{C}_{U_{2}}
. For every
u\in U_{2}
, we have
C_{u}\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}\cap\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}}
. Thus by the Riemann–Roch formula for Gorenstein curves
[
Sta25
,
Tag 0BS6
]
we have

h^{0}(C_{u},\omega_{C_{u}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C_{u}}(-1))=h^{1}(C_{u},\mathcal{O}_{C_{u}}(1))=h^{0}(C_{u},\mathcal{O}_{C_{u}}(1))-\chi(C_{u},\mathcal{O}_{C_{u}}(1))\geq g-(g-1)=1.

Clearly, the equality holds if
u\in H_{\rib}
as
\mathcal{F}
is relatively trivial over
H_{\rib}
. By the semicontinuity theorem we know that there exists an open neighborhood
U_{2}^{\prime}
of
H_{\rib}
in
U_{2}
such that
u\in U_{2}^{\prime}
if and only if
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=1
. In particular, for every
u\in U_{3}
there is a non-zero homomorphism
s_{u}:\mathcal{O}_{C_{u}}(1)\to\omega_{C_{u}}
that is unique up to scaling. Since
s_{u}
is clearly an isomorphism for
u\in H_{\rib}
, by openness of isomorphisms we know that there exists an open neighborhood
U_{3}
of
H_{\rib}
in
U_{2}^{\prime}
such that
u\in U_{3}
if and only if
\mathcal{O}_{C_{u}}(1)\cong\omega_{C_{u}}
.

Since every curve
C_{u}
in
U_{2}
is non-degenerate, this implies that
u\in U_{3}
if and only if
C_{u}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
is given by the canonical embedding. Thus we verified the condition of
U_{3}
.

Next, we show that there exists an open neighborhood
U_{5}
of
H_{\rib}
in
U_{3}
such that
u\in U_{5}\setminus H_{\rib}
if and only if
C_{u}
is reduced and
[C_{u}]
is Chow semistable. Let
U_{4}
be the open subscheme of
U_{3}
parameterizing curves
C
whose corresponding
1
-cycle is Chow semistable. Then
U_{4}
contains
H_{\rib}
. By the openness of reducedness we know that the locus
Z_{4}
parameterizing non-reduced curves in
U_{4}
is closed in
U_{4}
. We claim that
H_{\rib}
is a connected component of
Z_{4}
. By Lemmas
2.16
and
4.7
we know that
H_{\rib}
is closed in
Z_{4}
. By Theorem
3.9
and Proposition
3.10
we know that
H_{\rib}\to Z_{\rib}
is a Severi–Brauer scheme with geometric fibers isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1)))
. Thus
H_{\rib}
is connected by the connectedness of
Z_{\rib}
. Assume that there is a flat family of non-reduced curves
\{C_{t}\}_{t\in T\setminus o}
in
U_{4}
degenerating to
C_{o}
in
H_{\rib}
where
o\in T
is a smooth pointed curve. Then, the non-reduced
1
-cycles
\Phi_{g,1}(C_{t})
degenerate to the doubled rational normal curve. Thus after shrinking
T
we know that
\Phi_{g,1}(C_{t})
is the sum of at most two integral curves, which implies that
\Phi_{g,1}(C_{t})=2[(C_{t})_{\mathrm{red}}]
and
(C_{t})_{\mathrm{red}}
is integral. Let
C_{o}^{\prime}
be the flat degeneration of
(C_{t})_{\mathrm{red}}
as
t\to o
. Then we have

\chi(\mathcal{O}_{C_{o}^{\prime}})=\chi(\mathcal{O}_{(C_{t})_{\mathrm{red}}})=1-p_{a}(\mathcal{O}_{(C_{t})_{\mathrm{red}}})\leq 1.

On the other hand, it is clear that
C_{o}^{\prime}
is generically reduced with
(C_{o}^{\prime})_{\mathrm{red}}=(C_{o})_{\mathrm{red}}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Thus we have

\chi(\mathcal{O}_{C_{o}^{\prime}})\geq\chi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}})=1.

Combining the above two inequalities we conclude that
p_{a}(\mathcal{O}_{(C_{t})_{\mathrm{red}}})=0
which implies that
(C_{t})_{\mathrm{red}}
is a rational normal curve in
\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
. Thus Lemma
4.7
implies that
C_{t}
is a canonical ribbon. Thus the claim is proved, and we take
U_{5}:=U_{4}\setminus(Z_{4}\setminus H_{\rib})
.

To summarize, we have found an open subscheme
U_{5}\subset\Hilb_{g,1}
containing
H_{\rib}
such that
[C\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{g-1}]\in U_{5}
if and only if the following conditions hold.

•

C
has lci singularities;

•

\omega_{C}
is very ample and induces the closed embedding
C\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
;

•

the corresponding Chow
1
-cycle
[C]
is Chow semistable;

•

C
is either a canonical ribbon (i.e.
C\in H_{\rib}
) or reduced.

Next, we show that
U_{5}
is smooth.
Indeed, the above arguments actually imply that
U_{5}
is open in
\bm{H}_{\chi_{g}}(\mathbb{P}^{g-1})
. Thus standard arguments of moduli stacks being represented by a quotient stack of subschemes of Hilbert schemes implies that
[U_{5}/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]\to\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}\cap\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}}
is an open immersion such that every curve
[C]\in U_{5}\setminus H_{\rib}
satisfies
[C]\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
. Thus the smoothness of
U_{5}
follows from the smoothness of
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
and the unobstructedness of deformations of ribbons (Corollary
3.18
).

Finally, we define
\widetilde{U}_{g,1}:=U_{5}
. Then it remains to verify the properties (1)-(4) from the statement. (1) is automatic from the construction. For (2), it suffices to show that
U_{g,1}
is open in
\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}
as clearly it is contained in
\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}^{\rm ss}
by construction.
Since
\widetilde{U}_{g,1}
is smooth, we identify it with its seminormalization. By the properness of
\Phi_{g,1}:\Hilb_{g,1}^{\rm sn}\to\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}
, we know that
\Phi_{g,1}(\Hilb_{g,1}^{\rm sn}\setminus\widetilde{U}_{g,1})
is closed in
\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}
. Thus it suffices to show that
\Phi_{g,1}^{-1}(U_{g,1})=\widetilde{U}_{g,1}
. Suppose
[C^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{g-1}]\in\Phi_{g,1}^{-1}(U_{g,1})
, i.e. there exists a curve
C\in\widetilde{U}_{g,1}
such that
[C^{\prime}]=[C]
as
1
-cycles. If
C
is reduced, then we know that
C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}=C
which implies that
C^{\prime}=C
as they have the same Hilbert polynomial. If
C
is non-reduced, then from the definition of
U_{5}
we know that
C
is a canonical ribbon. Thus by Lemma
4.7
we know that
C^{\prime}
is also a canonical ribbon which implies that
C^{\prime}\in\widetilde{U}_{g,1}
. This verifies (2).

The first part of (3) is automatic from the construction. For the second part of (3), notice that
\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
contains
\mathcal{M}_{g}
as a dense open scheme. Hence any curve
C
satisfying the assumptions is smoothable which implies that the canonical image of
C
belongs to
\Hilb_{g,1}
. Then (3) follows from the construction. The first part of (4) is clear from the properness of
\Phi_{g,1}
. The second part of (4) follows from the fact that
\widetilde{U}_{g,1}\setminus H_{\rib}
only contains reduced curves.
For the last part of (4),
since
H_{\rib}\to Z_{\rib}
has connected fibers, by Zariski’s main theorem, seminormality of
U_{g,1}
, and smoothness of
\widetilde{U}_{g,1}
, we conclude that
U_{g,1}
is normal. This also implies that
\widetilde{U}_{g,1}\setminus H_{\rib}\to U_{g,1}\setminus Z_{\rib}
is an isomorphism. The proof is finished.
∎

4.4.
A specific open substack of
\mathscr{X}_{g}

In this subsection, we introduce an open substack
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
of
\mathscr{X}_{g}
. Later in the next subsection we will show that
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
admits a good moduli space.

Definition-Proposition 4.9
.

There exists an open substack
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}_{g}
parameterizing curves
C
of genus
g
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

C
is smoothable and has lci singularities;

(2)

\omega_{C}
is ample and basepoint free and induces the canonical map
\varphi:C\to\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
with
\varphi_{*}[C]
Chow semistable;

(3)

the Chow polystable degeneration of the
1
-cycle
\varphi_{*}[C]
is either a doubled rational normal curve or a reduced
1
-cycle
[C_{0}]
as the cycle-theoretic canonical image of a curve
C_{0}\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
with
\omega_{C_{0}}
very ample.

Proof.

Recall that
\phi_{g}^{\rm c}:\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c}=[\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}^{\rm ss}/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]\to\mathfrak{X}_{g}^{\rm c}
is a good moduli space morphism. By universal closedness of good moduli space morphisms
[
Alp13
, Theorem 4.16(ii)]
, we know that
\phi_{g}^{\rm c}([(\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}^{\rm ss}\setminus U_{g,1})/\mathrm{PGL}(g)])
is closed in
\mathfrak{X}_{g}^{\rm c}
, where
U_{g,1}
is obtained from Proposition
4.8
. We define

\mathscr{X}_{g}^{{\rm c},\circ}:=(\phi_{g}^{\rm c})^{-1}(\mathfrak{X}_{g}^{\rm c}\setminus\phi_{g}^{\rm c}([(\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}^{\rm ss}\setminus U_{g,1})/\mathrm{PGL}(g)])).

We also let
V_{g,1}
be the open subscheme of
\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}^{\rm ss}
such that
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{{\rm c},\circ}=[V_{g,1}/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]
.
The above definition is equivalent to saying that a Chow semistable
1
-cycle belongs to
V_{g,1}
if and only if its Chow polystable degeneration belongs to
U_{g,1}
.
Then from the definition we know that
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{{\rm c},\circ}
is the largest saturated open substack of
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c}
contained in
[U_{g,1}/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]
. To conclude our definition, let
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}_{g}
be the open substack such that

\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ,{\rm sn}}:=(\Phi_{g}^{\rm sn})^{-1}(\mathscr{X}_{g}^{{\rm c},\circ}).

Next, we verify that
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
satisfies the statement.
We start from showing that every curve
C
satisfying conditions (1)(2)(3) belongs to
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
. Since (1) and (2) guarantee that
C\in\mathscr{X}_{g}
, it suffices to show that the Chow
1
-cycle
\varphi_{*}[C]\in\mathscr{X}_{g}^{{\rm c},\circ}
. This is equivalent to showing that the Chow polystable degeneration of
\varphi_{*}[C]
belongs to
U_{g,1}
, which follows directly from condition (3) and Proposition
4.8
(3).

Finally, we show that every curve in
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
satisfies conditions (1)(2)(3). Note that every curve in
\mathscr{X}_{g}
is smoothable and satisfies (2) by definition. Thus it suffices to show that every curve
C
in
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
has lci singularities and satisfies (3). If
\varphi_{*}[C]
is a doubled rational normal curve (which implies (3)), then by Lemma
4.10
we know that
C
is either a hyperelliptic curve or a ribbon. Thus
C
has lci singularities.
If
\varphi_{*}[C]
is not a doubled rational normal curve, then we claim that
\varphi
is a closed embedding. Given the claim, we have
\varphi(C)\in\Hilb_{g,1}
whose corresponding
1
-cycle
\varphi_{*}[C]\in V_{g,1}\subset U_{g,1}
. Then by Proposition
4.8
we know that
[C]\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
. From the definition of
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{{\rm c},\circ}
and
V_{g,1}
, we know that the Chow polystable degeneration
[C_{0}]
of
\varphi_{*}[C]
belongs to
U_{g,1}
as well. Then by Proposition
4.8
(3) we know that either
[C_{0}]
is a doubled rational normal curve or
C_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
is the canonical embedding of a curve
C_{0}\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
with
\omega_{C_{0}}
very ample. Thus (3) is verified.

It remains to verify the claim.
By Proposition
4.8
(3) we know that
\varphi_{*}[C]\in U_{g,1}\setminus Z_{\rib}
is reduced. Denote by
\widetilde{V}_{g,1}:=\widetilde{U}_{g,1}\times_{U_{g,1}}V_{g,1}
. By surjectivity of
\widetilde{U}_{g,1}\to U_{g,1}
from Proposition
4.8
(4) there exists a curve
C^{\prime}
in
\widetilde{V}_{g,1}
such that
[C^{\prime}]=\varphi_{*}[C]
as
1
-cycles. Thus both
C
and
C^{\prime}
are reduced, and
\varphi:C\to C^{\prime}
is finite and birational. As a result, we have
\mathcal{O}_{C^{\prime}}\subset\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C}
whose cokernel
\mathcal{G}
has finite support, and hence

1-g=\chi(\mathcal{O}_{C^{\prime}})=\chi(\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C})-h^{0}(\mathcal{G})=\chi(\mathcal{O}_{C})-h^{0}(\mathcal{G})=1-g-h^{0}(\mathcal{G}).

Thus
h^{0}(\mathcal{G})=0
, i.e.
\mathcal{G}=0
, which implies that
\varphi:C\to C^{\prime}
is an isomorphism. Thus
\varphi
is a closed embedding and the claim is proved.
∎

The following lemma was used in the above proof.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
C\in\mathscr{X}_{g}(\mathbbm{k})
be a curve. Let
\varphi:C\to\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
be the finite morphism induced by the basepoint free linear system
|\omega_{C}|
. Then the
1
-cycle
\varphi_{*}[C]
is a doubled rational normal curve in
\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
if and only if
C
is either a hyperelliptic curve or a ribbon.

Proof.

The backward direction is easy, see Propositions
3.4
and
3.10
. We shall focus on the forward direction.
We first treat the case where
C
is reduced. Denote by
2[C_{0}]
the
1
-cycle of doubled rational normal curve.
Then
\varphi
induces a finite morphism
\varphi_{\mathrm{red}}:C\to C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}=C_{0}
. Moreover,
\varphi_{*}[C]=2[C_{0}]
implies that
(\varphi_{\mathrm{red}})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{C}
has rank
2
on
C_{0}
. Thus we know that
C
is hyperelliptic by Definition
3.1
.

Next we assume that
C
is non-reduced. Since
C
is Gorenstein and hence Cohen–Macaulay, it has no embedded points. Thus
C
has a generically non-reduced irreducible component. On the other hand,
\varphi_{*}[C]=2[C_{0}]
implies that
C
is irreducible, has multiplicity
2
at its generic point, and
\varphi_{\mathrm{red}}:C_{\mathrm{red}}\to C_{0}
is birational. Thus we have
C_{\mathrm{red}}\cong C_{0}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We will show that
C
is a ribbon. Let
\mathcal{I}
be the nilradical sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{C}
. Since
C
has multiplicity
2
at its generic point, we know that
\mathcal{I}
has rank
1
as an
\mathcal{O}_{C_{\mathrm{red}}}
-module and that
\mathcal{I}^{2}
has finite support. Thus
h^{1}(C,\mathcal{I}^{2})=0
and from the standard long exact sequence we have

1=h^{0}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})=h^{0}(C,\mathcal{I}^{2})+h^{0}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathcal{I}^{2})\geq h^{0}(C,\mathcal{I}^{2})+1,

which implies
\mathcal{I}^{2}=0
. Moreover, since
H^{0}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})\cong H^{0}(C_{\mathrm{red}},\mathcal{O}_{C_{\mathrm{red}}})\cong\mathbbm{k}
, we know that the map
H^{0}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})\to H^{0}(C_{\mathrm{red}},\mathcal{O}_{C_{\mathrm{red}}})
is an isomorphism. This implies that
H^{0}(C,\mathcal{I})=0
, in particular
\mathcal{I}
is torsion free as an
\mathcal{O}_{C_{\mathrm{red}}}
-module. Therefore, we conclude that
\mathcal{I}
is locally free of rank
1
as an
\mathcal{O}_{C_{\mathrm{red}}}
-module which implies that
C
is a ribbon.
∎

Theorem 4.11
.

The stack
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
is smooth and contains
\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}
. Moreover, the following hold.

(1)

The Hilbert–Chow morphism
\Phi_{g}^{\circ}:\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\to\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c}
has an open image
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{{\rm c},\circ}
.

(2)

The open substack
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{{\rm c},\circ}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c}
is saturated and normal.

(3)

\Phi_{g}^{\circ}:\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\to\mathscr{X}_{g}^{{\rm c},\circ}
is isomorphic away from
\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}
, and
\Phi_{g}^{\circ}(\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g})
is the point representing a doubled rational normal curve.

Proof.

We first show that
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
is smooth. Let
[C]\in\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}(\mathbbm{k})
be a curve. Since
\mathscr{X}_{g}
is an open substack of an irreducible component of
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
is smooth at
[C]
. Let
\varphi:C\to\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
be the finite morphism induced by the basepoint free linear system
|\omega_{C}|
. If
\varphi_{*}[C]
is a doubled rational normal curve, then by Lemma
4.10
we know that
C
is either a hyperelliptic curve or a ribbon. By Corollary
3.18
we know that the stack
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
is smooth at
[C]
. If
\varphi_{*}[C]
is not a doubled rational normal curve, then from the proof of Definition-Proposition
4.9
we have
[C]\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
which implies the smoothness of
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
at
[C]
.

Next, we show (1). Since
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
is smooth, it can be identified with its seminormalization
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ,\rm sn}
where
\Phi_{g}^{\rm sn}
restricts and gives
\Phi_{g}^{\circ}:\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\to\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}
. It suffices to show that
\Phi_{g}^{\circ}
is surjective onto
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}
. Let
z\in V_{g,1}
be a
1
-cycle. If
z
is non-reduced, then by Proposition
4.8
(3) we know that
z
is a doubled rational normal curve. Thus there exists a canonical ribbon
C
whose associated
1
-cycle is
z
. Then it follows from Definition-Proposition
4.9
that
C\in\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
. If
z
is reduced, by Proposition
4.8
(3) we know that there exists a curve
C\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
with
\omega_{C}
very ample such that
z
is the cycle-theoretic canonical image of
C
. Then we know that
C\in\mathscr{X}_{g}
whose Hilbert–Chow image is
z
, which implies that
C\in\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
. Thus (1) is proved.

Since normality of
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}
follows from normality of
U_{g,1}
from Proposition
4.8
, and saturatedness has been shown earlier in the proof of Definition-Proposition
4.9
, (2) is proved.

Finally, we show (3). By Lemma
4.10
we know that
\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}
is contained in
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
and
\Phi_{g}^{\circ}(\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g})=\{z_{\rib}\}
, where
z_{\rib}
represents a doubled rational normal curve. It suffices to show that
\Phi_{g}^{\circ}
induces an isomorphism between
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\setminus(\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g})
and
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}\setminus\{z_{\rib}\}
. By Proposition
4.8
(4) we have

\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}\setminus\{z_{\rib}\}=[(V_{g,1}\setminus Z_{\rib})/\mathrm{PGL}_{g}]\cong[(\widetilde{V}_{g,1}\setminus H_{\rib})/\mathrm{PGL}_{g}].

Thus it suffices to show that
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\setminus(\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g})
is isomorphic to
[(\widetilde{V}_{g,1}\setminus H_{\rib})/\mathrm{PGL}_{g}]
. From the proof of Definition-Proposition
4.9
we know that there is an open immersion
\iota:[\widetilde{V}_{g,1}/\mathrm{PGL}_{g}]\hookrightarrow\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
. Since
\widetilde{V}_{g,1}\subset\widetilde{U}_{g,1}
, by Proposition
4.8
(3) we know that every curve
C
in
\widetilde{V}_{g,1}\setminus H_{\rib}
satisfies that
C
is reduced with very ample
\omega_{C}
. Thus by Lemma
4.10
we have
\iota([(\widetilde{V}_{g,1}\setminus H_{\rib})/\mathrm{PGL}_{g}])\subset\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\setminus(\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g})
. Conversely, let
[C]\in\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\setminus(\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g})
be a point. Then
\varphi_{*}[C]
is not a doubled rational normal curve by Lemma
4.10
. From the claim in the proof of Definition-Proposition
4.9
, we conclude that
\varphi
is a closed embedding and
\varphi(C)\in\widetilde{V}_{g,1}
. Clearly
C
is not a canonical ribbon, which implies that
\varphi(C)\in\widetilde{V}_{g,1}\setminus H_{\rib}
. The proof is finished.
∎

4.5.
Existence of good moduli spaces

Let
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
be a smooth open substack of
\mathscr{X}_{g}
from Definition-Proposition
4.9
whose Hilbert–Chow image is denoted by
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}
.
Then
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}=[V_{g,1}/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]
admits a good moduli space
\mathfrak{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}=V_{g,1}\sslash\mathrm{PGL}(g)
as an open subscheme of
\mathfrak{X}_{g}^{\rm c}=\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}^{\rm ss}\sslash\mathrm{PGL}(g)
by saturatedness, where
V_{g,1}
is the
\mathrm{PGL}(g)
-equivariant open subscheme of
\mathbf{Chow}_{g,1}^{\rm ss}
from the proof of Definition-Proposition
4.9
. Denote by
\phi_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}:\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}\to\mathfrak{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}
the good moduli space morphism as the restriction of
\phi_{g}^{\rm c}:\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c}\to\mathfrak{X}_{g}^{\rm c}
. Let
\phi_{g}^{\circ}=\phi_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}\circ\Phi_{g}^{\circ}:\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\to\mathfrak{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}
be the composition.

Our goal in this subsection is to prove the following result.

Theorem 4.12
.

Notation as above. Then
\phi_{g}^{\circ}:\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\to\mathfrak{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}
is a good moduli space morphism.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{C}_{\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}}\to\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
be the universal family of curves. We have a vector bundle
\pi_{*}\omega_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}}/\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}}
of rank
g
on
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
. Let
\mathscr{Y}_{g}\to\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
be the
\mathrm{PGL}(g)
-torsor associated to
\pi_{*}\omega_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}}/\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}}
. Then we have a Hilbert–Chow morphism
\widetilde{\Phi}_{g}:\mathscr{Y}_{g}\to V_{g,1}
defined by
\widetilde{\Phi}(C,[s])=(\varphi_{s})_{*}[C]
, where
s=(s_{1},\cdots,s_{g})
is a basis of
H^{0}(C,\omega_{C})
, and
\varphi_{s}:C\to\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
is the finite morphism induced by
s
. Clearly
\widetilde{\Phi}_{g}
induces the morphism
\Phi_{g}^{\circ}:\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\to\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}=[V_{g,1}/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]
by taking the
\mathrm{PGL}(g)
-quotient. Let
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{g}:=\mathscr{H}_{g}\times_{\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}}\mathscr{Y}_{g}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g}:=\mathscr{R}_{g}\times_{\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}}\mathscr{Y}_{g}
denote the
\mathrm{PGL}(g)
-torsors over the closed substacks
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}
of
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}
respectively by Proposition
4.5
. Recall that
Z_{\rib}
is the closed
\mathrm{PGL}(g)
-orbit in
V_{g,1}
parameterizing doubled rational normal curves.

Lemma 4.13
.

Notation as above. Then
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\rib}:=\widetilde{\Phi}_{g}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{g}\cup\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g}}:\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{g}\cup\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g}\to Z_{\rib}
is a good moduli space morphism.

Proof.

We shall use the quotient presentations of stacks
\mathscr{H}_{g}
and
\mathscr{R}_{g}
from Theorems
3.14
and
3.15
:

\mathscr{H}_{g}\cong[\mathbb{A}(H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\otimes-2}))/G]\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathscr{R}_{g}\cong[\mathbb{A}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},L))/G],

where
G:=\mathrm{GL}(2)/\bm{\mu}_{g+1}
and
L:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1)
. By Proposition
4.6
we know that
\mathscr{H}_{g}
intersects
\mathscr{R}_{g}
at a unique point
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
which is the origin in both quotient presentations, where the intersection is transversal. Thus we have that

\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}\cong[(\mathbb{A}(H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\otimes-2}))\cup\mathbb{A}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},L)))/G],

where the union of the two affine spaces is transversal at the origin and hence an affine scheme. As a result, we know that
\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}\to\mathrm{B}G
is an affine morphism. Composing with the natural morphism
\mathrm{B}G\to\mathrm{B}\mathrm{PGL}(2)
where
G\to\mathrm{PGL}(2)
is the quotient group homomorphism, we see that
\Phi_{\rib}:\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}\to\rm B\mathrm{PGL}(2)
is cohomologically affine. Moreover,
\Phi_{\rib}
is the descent of the good moduli space morphism

[(\mathbb{A}(H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\otimes-2}))\cup\mathbb{A}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},L)))/(\mathbb{G}_{m}/\bm{\mu}_{g+1})]\to\Spec\mathbbm{k}

under a
\mathrm{PGL}(2)
-quotient.
Thus we conclude that
\Phi_{\rib}:\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}\to\rm B\mathrm{PGL}(2)
is a good moduli space morphism. By taking
\mathrm{PGL}(g)
-torsors of
\Phi_{\rib}
, we conclude that
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\rib}
is also a good moduli space morphism.
∎

Proposition 4.14
.

Notation as above. Then
\widetilde{\Phi}_{g}:\mathscr{Y}_{g}\to V_{g,1}
is a good moduli space morphism.

Proof.

We first show that
\mathscr{Y}_{g}
admits a good moduli space using Theorem
2.4
.

We start from checking condition (1) of Theorem
2.4
. Let
y\in\mathscr{Y}_{g}(\mathbbm{k})
be a closed point. If
y\not\in\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}
, then by the last paragraph of the proof of Theorem
4.11
there is a stack isomorphism
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}\setminus(\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g})\cong[(\widetilde{V}_{g,1}\setminus H_{\rib})/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]
. Thus by taking
\mathrm{PGL}(g)
-torsors we have an isomorphism
\mathscr{Y}_{g}\setminus(\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{g}\cup\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g})\cong\widetilde{V}_{g,1}\setminus H_{\rib}
. Moreover, by Proposition
4.8
(4) we know that
\widetilde{\Phi}_{g}^{\prime}=\widetilde{\Phi}_{g}|_{\mathscr{Y}_{g}\setminus(\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{g}\cup\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g})}:\mathscr{Y}_{g}\setminus(\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{g}\cup\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g})\to V_{g,1}\setminus Z_{\rib}
is an isomorphism, which implies that
\mathscr{Y}_{g}\setminus(\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{g}\cup\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g})
is represented by a scheme. Since
[(V_{g,1}\setminus Z_{\rib})/\mathrm{PGL}(g)]
is saturated open in
\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\rm c,\circ}
, every
\mathbbm{k}
-point in
\mathscr{Y}_{g}\setminus(\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{g}\cup\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g})
is closed. Thus (1) holds at
y\not\in\mathscr{H}_{g}\cup\mathscr{R}_{g}
.

If
y\in\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{g}\cup\widetilde{\mathscr{R}}_{g}
is a closed point, then by Proposition
4.6
we know that
y
parameterizes the hyperelliptic ribbon
R_{\mathrm{hyp}}
with a projective basis
[s]=[s_{1},\cdots,s_{g}]
of
H^{0}(R_{\mathrm{hyp}},\omega_{R_{\mathrm{hyp}}})
. By Proposition
3.16
we have
\Aut(R_{\mathrm{hyp}})\cong\mathrm{GL}(2)/\bm{\mu}_{g+1}.

Thus we have

\mathrm{Stab}_{y}\cong\Aut(R_{\mathrm{hyp}},[s])\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}/\bm{\mu}_{g+1}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}.

By Propositions
3.19
and
4.6
we have

T_{\mathscr{Y}_{g},y}\cong H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\otimes-2})\oplus\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{1}},L)\oplus T_{\rm id}\mathrm{PGL}(g+1)/\mathrm{PGL}(2).

Here
L=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-g-1)
. Hence
\mathrm{Stab}_{y}\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
T_{\mathscr{Y}_{g},y}
with weight
(2,-1,0)
in the above direct sum presentation. Thus we see that the locus of vectors in
T_{\mathscr{Y},y}
with non-trivial stabilizers are precisely the union of
(T_{\rm id}\mathrm{PGL}(g+1)/\mathrm{PGL}(2))\setminus\{0\}
(with stabilizer
\mathbb{G}_{m}
) and
H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},L^{\otimes-2})\setminus\{0\}
(with stabilizer
\bm{\mu}_{2}
). By Theorem
2.3
, there exists a local quotient presentation
