(S_{2A_{5}},C_{2A_{5}}^{\circ}
) as a specialization of
(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1},C_{2A_{5}})
.

Lemma 6.4
.

The
\mathbb{Q}
-Gorenstein deformation of
S_{2A_{5}}
is unobstructed.
Moreover, every small
\mathbb{Q}
-Gorenstein deformation of
S_{2A_{5}}
is isomorphic to
S_{2A_{5}}
or
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Proof.

We will show that our surface
S_{2A_{5}}
is the same surface that is parameterized by
\xi_{3,3}
in
[
DH21
, Theorem 1]
. From here, by
[
DH21
, Theorem 6.6]
, the
\mathbb{Q}
-Gorenstein deformations of the surface
S_{2A_{5}}
are unobstructed. Furthermore, by
[
DH21
, Theorem 1 (4)]
, the locus of pairs
(S,C)
is a divisor, and so the only possible deformations are
S
or
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

By Definition
6.2
, the surface
S_{2A_{5}}
is constructed via gluing two copies
S_{A_{5}}
where each
S_{A_{5}}
is obtained via (1,3)-weighted blowup of
\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
at the point
[1,0,0]
. We recall that the fan of
\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
is generated in appropriate coordinates by primitive vectors
\mathbf{v}_{1}=(0,1),\mathbf{v}_{2}=(1,0)
and
\mathbf{v}_{3}=(-1,-3)
. The fan of the weighted blowup is obtained by forming the star subdivision associated to the (1,3)-weighted blowup, i.e. adding the vector
3\mathbf{v}_{2}+\mathbf{v}_{3}
(see e.g.
[
KM92
, 10.3]
). This construction agrees with the description of the surface parameterized by
\xi_{3,3}
in
[
DH21
]
, see
[
DH21
, Remark 5.14 and Figure 9]
for the toric description. ∎

Lemma 6.5
.

If
[(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
, then
D
is Cartier and has lci singularities.

Proof.

Because
[(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
, by definition it admits a smoothing to
(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{2}{3}D^{\prime})
where
D^{\prime}
is a
(3,3)
curve. By
[
ABB+23
, Lemma 2.11]
, because
3K_{\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}+2D^{\prime}\sim 0
, we have
3K_{S_{2A_{5}}}+2D\sim 0
and hence
2D\sim-3K_{S_{2A_{5}}}
where
\sim
denotes linear equivalence. From the explicit description of
S_{2A_{5}}
,
3K_{S_{2A_{5}}}
is Cartier and hence
2D
is Cartier. As the local singularities of
S_{2A_{5}}
are either normal crossing or of the form
(xy=0)\subset\frac{1}{3}(1,1,2)
, any integral
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisor
\Delta
on
S_{2A_{5}}
satisfies
3\Delta
is Cartier and therefore
3D
is Cartier. Because
3D
is Cartier and
2D
is Cartier,
D
is Cartier. Now, we verify that
D
has lci singularities. Because
[(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
, the pair
(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}D)
has slc singularities and hence by
[
Kol23
, Definition-Lemma 5.10]
, the normalization
(S_{2A_{5}}^{\nu},\frac{2}{3}D^{\nu}+\ell)
has log canonical singularities. This implies that
D
does not contain the double locus of
S_{2A_{5}}
. Write
S_{2A_{5}}=S_{A_{5}}\cup S_{A_{5}}
and let
(S_{A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}D_{1}+\ell)
be either component of the normalization. Because
D
is Cartier,
D_{1}
is Cartier. If
D_{1}
passes through the
\frac{1}{3}(1,1)
singularity, it necessarily has multiplicity at least three at that point, in which case
(S_{A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}D_{1}+\ell)
is not log canonical. Therefore,
D_{1}
avoids the
\frac{1}{3}(1,1)
singularity. By restricting to one of the components, this implies that
D
avoids the
\frac{1}{3}(1,1,2)
singularities on
S_{2A_{5}}
. In particular,
D
is a Cartier divisor that only meets the normal crossing locus of
S_{2A_{5}}
and therefore has lci singularities.
∎

Proposition 6.6
.

Suppose
[(S,\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}\setminus\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
admits a special degeneration to
(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}C_{2A_{5}}^{\circ})
. Then
S\cong S_{2A_{5}}
and
D
is defined by the strict transform of the equation

\displaystyle(x_{2}^{2}-x_{0}^{3}x_{2}+ax_{0}x_{1}^{2}x_{2}+b_{0}x_{1}^{6}+b_{1}x_{0}x_{1}^{5}+b_{2}x_{0}^{2}x_{1}^{4}+b_{3}x_{0}^{3}x_{1}^{3}=0)\subset\mathbb{P}(1,1,3);

\displaystyle(x_{2}^{\prime 2}-x_{0}^{\prime 3}x_{2}^{\prime}+a^{\prime}x_{0}^{\prime}x_{1}^{\prime 2}x_{2}^{\prime}+b_{0}^{\prime}x_{1}^{\prime 6}+b_{1}^{\prime}x_{0}^{\prime}x_{1}^{\prime 5}+b_{2}^{\prime}x_{0}^{\prime 2}x_{1}^{\prime 4}+b_{3}^{\prime}x_{0}^{\prime 3}x_{1}^{\prime 3}=0)\subset\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)

on each component of
S_{2A_{5}}
.
Here
([a,b_{0},b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}],[a^{\prime},b_{0}^{\prime},b_{1}^{\prime},b_{2}^{\prime},b_{3}^{\prime}])\in\mathbb{P}(2,6,5,4,3)^{2}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.4
we know that
S\cong S_{2A_{5}}
or
S\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. The latter is not possible as otherwise
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
is K-polystable which implies
[(S,\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
by interpolation
[
ADL24
, Proposition 2.13]
, a contradiction. Since there is a symmetry between
S_{A_{5}}
and
S_{A_{5}}^{\prime}
, we may focus on the curve
D_{1}:=D|_{S_{A_{5}}}
. Denote by
C_{1}:=\pi_{*}D_{1}
and
D_{0}:=C_{2A_{5}}^{\circ}|_{S_{A_{5}}}
. Then we know that
C_{1}
specially degenerates to the curve
C_{0}:=(x_{2}^{2}-x_{0}^{3}x_{2}=0)
. This degeneration is induced by a non-trivial power
\sigma^{m}
of the
1
-PS
\sigma
in
\Aut(S_{A_{5}},\ell_{1}+\frac{2}{3}D_{1})
such that
\pi_{*}\sigma
acts on
\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
as
\pi_{*}\sigma(t)\cdot[x_{0},x_{1},x_{2}]=[tx_{0},x_{1},t^{3}x_{2}]
. Suppose
C_{1}=(f(x_{0},x_{2})+x_{1}g(x_{0},x_{1},x_{2})=0)
. We claim that
m>0
and
g\neq 0
. If
g=0
then clearly
C_{1}
is
\pi_{*}\sigma
-invariant, which implies that
\Aut(S_{2A_{5}},D)
is infinite, a contradiction to KSBA stability. It is clear that

C_{0}=\lim_{t\to 0}\pi_{*}\sigma^{m}(t)\cdot C_{1}=\begin{cases}(f(x_{0},x_{2})=0)&\textrm{ if }m>0;\\
(x_{1}h(x_{0},x_{1},x_{2})=0)&\textrm{ if }m<0.\end{cases}

Here
h
is the sum of terms in
g
with the highest degree in
x_{1}
.
Since
C_{0}
does not contain the line
(x_{1}=0)
, we must have
m>0
.
Thus after rescaling we have
f(x_{0},x_{2})=x_{2}^{2}-x_{0}^{3}x_{2}
.

Moreover, if we denote by
v
the monomial valuation centered at
[1,0,0]
of weight
(1,3)
in
(x_{1},x_{2})
, then the condition
D_{1}\sim D_{0}
implies that
v(C_{1})=v(C_{0})=3
. Thus the terms
x_{0}^{5}x_{1}
and
x_{0}^{4}x_{1}^{2}
do not appear in the equation of
C_{1}
. Moreover, using the affine transformation
[x_{0},x_{1},x_{2}]\mapsto[x_{0}+rx_{1},x_{1},x_{2}+sx_{1}^{3}]
we can eliminate the terms
x_{0}^{2}x_{1}x_{2}
and
x_{1}^{3}x_{2}
in the equation of
C_{1}
. As a result, we get the desired form of
C_{1}
. Moreover, by projection to
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{[x_{0},x_{1}]}
we know that
C_{1}
is a hyperelliptic curve of arithmetic genus
2
. By taking the discriminant it is not hard to see that
C_{1}
has at worst
A_{4}
-singularities if and only if
a,b_{0},b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}
are not all zero. Thus we know that
(S_{2A_{5}},(\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon)D)
is KSBA stable if and only if both vectors
(a,b_{0},b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})
and
(a^{\prime},b_{0}^{\prime},b_{1}^{\prime},b_{2}^{\prime},b_{3}^{\prime})
are non-zero. Thus the proof is finished.
∎

We summarize the behavior of the bpCY wall crossing near
[(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}C^{\circ}_{2A_{5}})]
in the following proposition.

Proposition 6.7
.

Consider the projective morphisms induced by the bpCY wall crossing

\overline{M}^{\mathrm{K}}_{4}\rightarrow\overline{M}^{\mathrm{CY}}_{4}\leftarrow\overline{M}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}_{4}.

Let
[(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}C^{\circ}_{2A_{5}})]
be a polystable point of
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
from Proposition
6.3
. The preimage of this pair in
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
is the pair
[(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{2}{3}C_{2A_{5}})]
(see Lemma
6.1
), and the morphism
\overline{M}^{\mathrm{K}}_{4}\to\overline{M}^{\mathrm{CY}}_{4}
is an isomorphism on the preimage of an open neighborhood of
[(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}C^{\circ}_{2A_{5}})]
.

Proof.

Firstly we claim that
x:=[(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{2}{3}C_{2A_{5}})]
is a connected component in the preimage of
y:=[(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}C^{\circ}_{2A_{5}})]
. By openness of K-semistability and the Paul–Tian criterion
[
ADL24
, Theorem 2.22]
, there is an isomorphism between some open neighborhood of
x
in
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
and some open neighborhood of the point
[C_{2A_{5}}]
in the GIT moduli space of
(3,3)
-curves on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Then by
[
Fed12
]
we know that there exists an open neighborhood
U
of
x
in
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
that
U\setminus\{x\}
parameterizes klt bpCY pairs of the form
(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{2}{3}C)
where
C
has at worst
A_{4}
-singularities. This implies that the image of
U\setminus\{x\}
in
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
does not contain
y
. Hence the claim is proved.

Next, we consider a sufficiently small open neighborhood
V
of
y
in
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
whose preimage in
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
is denoted by
U^{\prime}
. By Proposition
2.32
,
V
is normal. Since the induced map
U^{\prime}\to V
is birational and proper, by Zariski’s Main Theorem we know that the preimage of
y
in
U^{\prime}
is connected. Thus the claim implies that the preimage of
y
in
U^{\prime}
is precisely
x
. Then after shrinking
V
we may assume that
U^{\prime}\subset U
and the map
U^{\prime}\to V
is birational and finite. Thus
U^{\prime}\to V
is an isomorphism by Zariski’s main theorem. The proof is finished.
∎

6.2.
Replacing
D_{4}

In the Chow moduli space, there is a unique polystable point with
D_{4}
singularities by
[
CMJL12
, Remark 3.3]
:
C_{D}=V(x_{0}x_{3},x_{1}^{3}+x_{2}^{3})
. We will consider this curve and its S-equivalent pairs in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
and to do, we first fix notation. We denote by
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
a smooth quadric and by
\mathbb{P}(1,1,2)
a singular quadric cone in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
with coordinates
[x_{0}:x_{1}:x_{2}:x_{3}]
. We denote by
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}:=V(x_{0}x_{3})\subset\mathbb{P}^{3}
the non-normal quadric surface that is the union of two planes.

Lemma 6.8
.

A small deformation of a (possibly singular) reduced quadric surface in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
is still a quadric surface.

Proof.

This follows directly from
[
Ser06
, Examples 3.2.11]
.
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

The pair
(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},\frac{2}{3}C_{D})
, depicted in Figure
1
, is polystable in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
.

Proof.

By a straightfoward computation,
(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},\frac{2}{3}C_{D})
is slc. There is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{2}
action on
(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},C_{D})
given by automorphisms of
\mathbb{P}^{3}
of the form
t\cdot[x_{0}:x_{1}:x_{2}:x_{3}]=[tx_{0}:x_{1}:x_{2}:x_{3}]
and
s\cdot[x_{0}:x_{1}:x_{2}:x_{3}]=[x_{0}:x_{1}:x_{2}:sx_{3}]
and hence by
[
ABB+23
, Proposition 8.11]
, the pair is polystable.
∎

Lemma 6.10
.

Suppose
[(X,\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
admits a special degeneration to
(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},\frac{2}{3}C_{D})
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. Then,
X
is a normal quadric surface and
D
has at least one
D_{4}
singularity in the smooth locus of
X
. The K-polystable points of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
admitting such a degeneration are, up to coordinate change, given by
X=V(x_{0}x_{3}+q(x_{1},x_{2}))\subset\mathbb{P}^{3}
, where
q
is a nonzero quadratic form, and
D=V(x_{1}^{3}+x_{2}^{3})|_{X}
. The locus of such pairs has dimension
2
in
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
.

Proof.

Normality of
X
follows as any pair
[(X,\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
satisfies
(X,(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)D)
is klt, and
X
must be a quadric surface by Lemma
6.8
. If
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)
is klt, it is stable in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
by
[
ABB+23
, Theorem 6.15]
and does not admit nontrivial special degenerations, so therefore
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)
must be strictly log canonical. As
D
is deformation of
C_{D}
, this is possible only if
D
has at least one
D_{4}
singularity in the smooth locus of
X
. Now, suppose
X
is the smooth quadric surface
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
with coordinates
[x:y]\times[u:v]
and suppose
D\subset X
is a
(3,3)
curve with a
D_{4}
singularity. Up to coordinate change, we may assume the
D_{4}
singularity is at the point
[1:0]\times[1:0]
and
D
has equation
y^{3}f_{3}(u,v)+xy^{2}vf_{2}(u,v)+x^{2}yv^{2}f_{1}(u,v)+x^{3}v^{3}f_{0}(u,v)
where
f_{i}\in H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}(i))
. Given any such
D
, it admits a special degeneration induced by the 1-parameter subgroup
t\cdot([x,y]\times[u,v])=[x,ty]\times[t^{-1}u,v]
to a curve
D_{0}
with equation
a_{3}y^{3}u^{3}+a_{2}xy^{2}u^{2}v+a_{1}x^{2}yuv^{2}+a_{0}x^{3}v^{3}
where
a_{i}
is the coefficient of
v^{3-i}
in
f_{i}
. As these are the lowest degree terms with respect to the weight of
y
and
v
, the condition that
D
has a
D_{4}
singularity is precisely that this equation has three distinct roots. Therefore, choosing an appropriate embedding of
D_{0}
into
\mathbb{P}^{3}
, we find that
(X,D_{0})
is defined by the equations in the proposition. A similar computation holds if
X
is the singular quadric cone.

To complete the proof, it suffices to show that the pairs
(X,\frac{2}{3}D_{0})
as above are K-polystable. By
[
Fed12
, Section 2.1]
, if
X
is a smooth quadric surface, then
D_{0}
is GIT polystable and the K-polystability of
(X,\frac{2}{3}D_{0})
follows from interpolation exactly as in the proof of Lemma
6.1
. If
X
is the singular quadric cone, by
[
CMJL14
, Theorem 6.3(4)]
,
D_{0}
is polystable in the VGIT moduli stack of
(2,3)
complete intersections for
t\in(\tfrac{2}{9},\tfrac{2}{5})
. By
[
ADL23
, Theorem 1.4]
,
(X,cD_{0})
is therefore K-polystable for
c\in(\tfrac{1}{6},\tfrac{1}{3})
and by interpolation as in Lemma
6.1
, we see that
(X,cD_{0})
is K-polystable for all
c\in(\tfrac{1}{6},\tfrac{2}{3})
.

Finally, we obtain the dimension count by observing that there is a
2
-dimensional choice of quadratic form
q(x_{1},x_{2})
up to scaling.
∎

Lemma 6.11
.

Suppose
[(X,\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}\setminus\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
admits a special degeneration to
(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},\frac{2}{3}C_{D})
. Then,
X\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}\subset\mathbb{P}^{3}
and
D=V(x_{1}^{3}+x_{2}^{3}+x_{0}f(x_{0},x_{1},x_{2})+x_{3}g(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}))|_{X}
, where
f,g
are nonzero polynomials of degree
2
. These parameterize curves on the union of two planes such that each component of the curve is a cubic plane curve with at worst
A_{3}
singularities meeting the intersection line of the planes in three fixed points. The locus of such pairs has codimension
3
in
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.8
,
X
must be a quadric surface. Furthermore, by Proposition
2.34
and semicontinuity of log canonical thresholds,
X
must be
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}
, otherwise
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
. On each component of
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}
, the curve
D
must be a cubic meeting the intersection line in three distinct points and it is straightforward to write
D
in the above form. Furthermore, the cubic plane curve on each component must have log canonical threshold strictly greater than
\frac{2}{3}
, and by classification of cubic curves can have at worst
A_{3}
singularities.

Finally, up to automorphisms of each plane fixing the line of intersection, there is a 3-dimensional space of curves on each plane and therefore the dimension of such pairs in
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
is 6 in the 9-dimensional space
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
.
∎

Summarizing the content of Lemmas
6.9
,
6.10
, and
6.11
, we have the following proposition describing the bpCY wall crossing in a neighborhood of
[(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},\frac{2}{3}C_{D})]
.

Proposition 6.12
.

Consider the projective morphisms induced by the bpCY wall crossing

\overline{M}^{\mathrm{K}}_{4}\rightarrow\overline{M}^{\mathrm{CY}}_{4}\leftarrow\overline{M}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}_{4}.

Let
[(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},\frac{2}{3}C_{D})]
be the polystable point of
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
from Lemma
6.9
. The preimage of
[(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},\frac{2}{3}C_{D})]
in
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
is the
2
-dimensional locus of polystable pairs in Lemma
6.10
. These curves have two
D_{4}
singularities. The preimage of
[(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},\frac{2}{3}C_{D})]
in
\overline{M}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}_{4}
is the codimension
3
locus of pairs in Lemma
6.11
. These curves are elliptic triboroughs, where each elliptic component has at worst
A_{3}
singularities.

6.3.
Smoothness of CY moduli stack

In this section, we prove that the stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
of bpCY pairs is smooth. This will be used repeatedly in verification of the remaining walls in the Hassett–Keel program.

The previous sections explicitly studies the polystable points
(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}C_{2A_{5}}^{\circ})
(Proposition
6.3
) and
(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},\frac{2}{3}C_{D})
(Lemma
6.9
) of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
corresponding to the curves
C_{2A_{5}}
and
C_{D}
of
\mathscr{X}^{\rm c}
. There is an additional polystable point of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
corresponding to the curves
C_{A,B}
.

Following Definition
2.18
, let
Q
denote the singular quadric cone
V(x_{2}^{2}-x_{1}x_{3})\subset\mathbb{P}^{3}
and
C_{A,B}
the curve
V(x_{2}^{2}-x_{1}x_{3},Ax_{1}^{3}+Bx_{0}x_{1}x_{2}+x_{0}^{2}x_{3})\subset\mathbb{P}^{3}
. Via the identification
Q=\mathbb{P}(1,1,2)
and using weighted coordinates
[x:y:z]
on
\mathbb{P}(1,1,2)
,
C_{A,B}
may be written as
V(Ax^{6}+Bx^{3}yz+y^{2}z^{2})\subset\mathbb{P}(1,1,2)
. Recall that, for
4A\neq B^{2}
, the curve
C_{A,B}
has an
A_{3}
singularity at the vertex of the cone and an
A_{5}
singularity at the smooth point
[0:1:0]
and observe that
\Aut(\mathbb{P}(1,1,2),C_{A,B})
contains
\mathbb{G}_{m}
as the automorphism
t\cdot[x:y:z]=[tx:y:t^{3}z]
.

Definition 6.13
.

Let
\mathbb{P}(1,2,3)\cup\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
be the surface
V(a_{1}a_{2})\subset\mathbb{P}(1,1,2,3)
where the weighted coordinates on
\mathbb{P}(1,1,2,3)
are
[a_{0}:a_{1}:a_{2}:a_{3}]
. Let
C_{A,B}^{\circ}
be the curve
V(a_{1}a_{2},Aa_{0}^{6}+Ba_{0}^{2}a_{3}+a_{3}^{2})
. For any
A,B
such that
4A\neq B^{2}
, observe that by coordinate change we have
C_{A,B}^{\circ}\cong C_{1,0}^{\circ}=V(a_{1}a_{2},a_{0}^{6}+a_{3}^{2})
.

Lemma 6.14
.

If
4A\neq B^{2}
, the pairs
(Q,C_{A,B})
admit a weakly special degeneration to the pair
(\mathbb{P}(1,2,3)\cup\mathbb{P}(1,1,3),C_{1,0}^{\circ})
, where
C_{1,0}^{\circ}
is defined above, and the point
[(\mathbb{P}(1,2,3)\cup\mathbb{P}(1,1,3),\frac{2}{3}C_{1,0}^{\circ})]
is a polystable point of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
.

Proof.

For fixed
A,B
, let
(\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{C}):=(Q,C_{A,B})\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}
be the trivial family of pairs over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. In the fiber
(t=0)
, let
x,z
be local coordinates near the
A_{5}
singularity of
C_{A,B}
. Perform a
(1,3,1)
weighted blow up in the local coordinates
(x,z,t)
. This yields a new family
(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
with central fiber
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{0}=\widetilde{Q}\cup\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
, where
\widetilde{Q}
is the
(1,3)
-weighted blow-up of the point
[0:1:0]\in Q
. Let
s_{z}\subset\widetilde{Q}
be the strict transform of the section
(z=0)\in Q
. By direct computation,
s_{z}
is a
-1
-curve in
\widetilde{Q}
and extremal in
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
with
K_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}\cdot s_{z}=0
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cdot s_{z}=0
. In particular, we may contract
s_{z}
in a small contraction to a new family
(\overline{\mathcal{Q}},\overline{\mathcal{C}})
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, a weakly special degeneration of
(Q,C_{A,B})
to
(\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{0},\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{0})
. As the contraction of
s_{z}
in
\widetilde{Q}
is a birational morphism
\widetilde{Q}\to\mathbb{P}(1,2,3)
, the central fiber
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}\cong\mathbb{P}(1,2,3)\cup\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
. From the equation of the strict transform of
C_{A,B}
, the curve
\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
is precisely
C_{A,B}^{\circ}
. As
C_{A,B}^{\circ}\cong C_{1,0}^{\circ}
, we have constructed the claimed weakly special degeneration. Now, observe that
C_{1,0}^{\circ}
has an
A_{5}
singularity at a smooth point of
\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
, an
A_{3}
singularity at the
\frac{1}{2}(1,1)
singularity of
\mathbb{P}(1,2,3)
, and meets the double locus of
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}
transversally. In particular, the pair
(\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{0},\tfrac{2}{3}C_{1,0}^{\circ})
has type II slc singularities and is a point of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
and the normalization is non-dlt. By
[
ABB+23
, Proposition 6.6]
,
\dim\Aut(\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{0},C_{1,0}^{\circ})>\dim\Aut(Q,C_{A,B})=1
and by
[
ABB+23
, Proposition 8.11]
,
\dim\Aut(\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{0},C_{1,0}^{\circ})\leq 2
, so
\dim\Aut(\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{0},C_{1,0}^{\circ})=2
and
(\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{0},C_{1,0}^{\circ})
is polystable by
[
ABB+23
, Proposition 8.11]
.
∎

Remark 6.15
.

We have now exhibited three non-isomorphic type II polystable points of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
with non-dlt normalization:
(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}C_{2A_{5}}^{\circ})
,
(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},\frac{2}{3}C_{D})
, and
(\mathbb{P}(1,2,3)\cup\mathbb{P}(1,1,3),\frac{2}{3}C_{1,0}^{\circ})
.

Theorem 6.16
.

The moduli stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is smooth. Moreover, its good moduli space
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is a normal projective variety and isomorphic to the Baily–Borel compactification of Kondō’s ball quotient from
[
Kon02
]
.

Proof.

Firstly, by
[
BL24
, Theorem 7.11]
we have an isomorphism between the normalization of
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
and the Baily–Borel compactification
F^{\rm BB}
, where
F
denotes Kondō’s ball quotient
\mathcal{B}/\Gamma
from
[
Kon02
]
. Any point of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is either of type I or type II by
[
ABB+23
, Proposition 8.18]
. Under this isomorphism, the type I pairs correspond to the interior points of
F^{\rm BB}
and the type II pairs correspond to the cusps. By
[
CMJL12
, Theorem 5.9]
, there are precisely three cusps in
F^{\rm BB}
, which then correspond to at most three polystable type II pairs in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. By Remark
6.15
, there are at least three such type II polystable points so the normalization
(\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}})^{\nu}\to\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
must be bijective over these points. However, these three pairs have unobstructed deformations by Proposition
2.32
, so
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is smooth in a neighborhood of each point and hence
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is normal in a neighborhood of each point. As all other points of
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
must correspond to interior points of
F^{\rm BB}
, they are of type I and hence klt pairs, and again by Proposition
2.32
have unobstructed deformations. Therefore,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is smooth at these points and
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is normal. In particular,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is smooth at all points. Because
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is normal, it is equal to its normalization and therefore isomorphic to
F^{\rm BB}
.
∎

6.4.
From Calabi–Yau pairs to curves

We use the results of the previous subsection to prove that the Hassett–Keel program in a neighborhood of
[C_{D}]
and
[C_{2A_{5}}]
can be obtained by bpCY wall crossing. In particular, we will prove that there is a forgetful map
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{C}urves^{\mathrm{lci}+}_{4}
that is an isomorphism over a dense open subset containing these points (see Theorem
6.18
).

Proposition 6.17
.

There exists a smooth open substack
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
that parameterizes bpCY pairs
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)
such that either
X\subset\mathbb{P}^{3}
is a quadric surface or
X\cong S_{2A_{5}}
. Moreover, every
\mathbbm{k}
-point
[(X,\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}(\mathbbm{k})
satisfies that
D
is connected, reduced, with lci singularities, and Cartier in
X
.

Proof.

To show openness of
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
, it suffices to show that the underlying surface being a quadric surface or isomorphic to
S_{2A_{5}}
is open in families of bpCY pairs. Clearly this condition is constructible, so we only need to show that it is preserved under generalization. Let
f:(X,\frac{2}{3}D)\to B
be a family of bpCY pairs in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
over a pointed smooth curve
0\in B
such that
X_{0}
is either a quadric surface or
X_{0}\cong S_{2A_{5}}
. In the latter case, by Lemma
6.4
we know that after shrinking
B
, every fiber
X_{b}
is either isomorphic to
S_{2A_{5}}
or isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. By Proposition
2.32
, we know that
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is smooth.

The reducedness of
D
follows from the fact that
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)
is slc. The connectedness of
D
follows from ampleness of
D
in
X
. If
X
is isomorphic to a quadric surface, then
D\sim\mathcal{O}_{X}(3)
is Cartier and hence
D
has lci singularities. If
X\cong S_{2A_{5}}
, this is the content of Lemma
6.5
.
∎

The main goal of this subsection is to prove the following result on the forgetful map.

Theorem 6.18
.

There is an open immersion of algebraic stacks
\Psi:\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}\to\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
induced by the forgetful map
[(X,D)]\mapsto[D]
.

Proposition 6.19
.

The map
\Psi
is well-defined as a morphism of algebraic stacks.

Proof.

Let
f:(X,\frac{2}{3}D)\to B
be a family of bpCY pairs over a scheme
B
from pulling back the universal family over
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. By
[
ABB+23
, Remark 3.2(vii)]
, we know that
D
is flat proper over
B
of relative pure dimension
1
. By Proposition
6.17
, we know that
D_{b}
has lci singularities hence is Gorenstein for each
b\in B
, and
h^{0}(D_{b},\mathcal{O}_{D_{b}})=1
as
D_{b}
is reduced. Hence
b\to\chi(\mathcal{O}_{D_{b}})
is locally constant and equals
-3
by irreducibility of
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. Thus
h^{1}(D_{b},\mathcal{O}_{D_{b}})=4
. By Proposition
6.17
, we know that every fiber
D_{b}
is connected, reduced, with lci singularities, in particular with finitely many singularities. This shows that
D\to B
is a flat proper family of curves from pulling back the universal family over
\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
. The proof is finished.
∎

Lemma 6.20
.

Let
D
and
D^{\prime}
be two curves on
S_{2A_{5}}
such that both
(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}D)
and
(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}D^{\prime})
belong to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. If
D\cong D^{\prime}
, then there exists
\sigma\in\Aut(S_{2A_{5}})
such that
D^{\prime}=\sigma^{*}D
.

Proof.

Recall that
S_{2A_{5}}
is the union of two copies of
S_{A_{5}}
, each the weighted blow-up of
\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
, glued along
\ell
the strict transform of the ruling through the point blown up. Denote the map by
\pi:S_{2A_{5}}\to\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)\cup\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
and the point blown up by
p
. By definition of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
, the curve
D
(respectively
D^{\prime}
) is the union of two genus 2 hyperelliptic curves meeting at a non-Weierstress point
q
(respectively,
q^{\prime}
), noting that the curves
D_{i},D_{i}^{\prime}
are indeed hyperelliptic as in Definition
3.1
via the composition of
\pi
and the projection map on
\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
. Write
D=D_{1}\cup D_{2}
(respectively,
D^{\prime}=D_{1}^{\prime}\cup D_{2}^{\prime}
) as the union of these hyperelliptic components.

As
q
(respectively,
q^{\prime}
) is a non-Weierstrass point of each
D_{i}
(respectively,
D_{i}^{\prime}
), an isomorphism
D\cong D^{\prime}
must fix
q
and
q^{\prime}
and be compatible with the hyperelliptic map
\phi:D_{i}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
(respectively,
\phi^{\prime}:D_{i}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
) and therefore fix the second point in the fiber
\phi^{-1}(q)
on each component
D_{i}
. Denote these points by
q_{1},q_{2}
(respectively,
q_{1}^{\prime},q_{2}^{\prime}
). Let
\overline{D}
(respectively
\overline{D^{\prime}})
be the image of
D
(respectively
D^{\prime}
) in the map
\pi:S_{2A_{5}}\to\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)\cup\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
, which by construction glues the points
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
(respectively
q_{1}^{\prime}
and
q_{2}^{\prime}
). As any isomorphism
D\cong D^{\prime}
must fix the points
q_{1},q_{2}
(respectively,
q_{1}^{\prime},q_{2}^{\prime}
), is it equivalent to an isomorphism
\overline{D}\cong\overline{D^{\prime}}
. By Corollary
3.5
, as any two isomorphic genus 2 hyperelliptic curves differ by an automorphism of
\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
, it follows that the isomorphism
\overline{D}\cong\overline{D^{\prime}}
extends to an element
\overline{\sigma}\in\Aut(\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)\cup\mathbb{P}(1,1,3))
fixing
p
and
q
(respectively,
q^{\prime}
) such that
\overline{\sigma}^{*}\overline{D}=\overline{D^{\prime}}
. Then, if
\sigma=\pi\circ\overline{\sigma}
, the isomorphism
D\cong D^{\prime}
is realized by
D^{\prime}=\sigma^{*}D
.
∎

Corollary 6.21
.

The morphism
\Psi
is stabilizer preserving, in particular representable.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
\Psi
induces an isomorphism
\Aut(X,D)\xrightarrow{\cong}\Aut(D)
. If
X
is isomorphic to a quadric surface
Q
, we know that
D
is a complete
(2,3)
-intersection curve in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
. Thus this follows from the fact that
Q
is the unique quadric surface containing
D
and that
H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{3},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{3}}(1))\to H^{0}(D,\mathcal{O}_{D}(1))
is an isomorphism. If
X
is isomorphic to
S_{2A_{5}}
, by Lemma
6.20
, an automorphism of
D
can be lifted to a unique automorphism of
X
fixing
\Delta_{i}
, the exceptional divisor of the weighted blow-up on each component
S_{A_{5}}
.

By computation,
\Delta_{i}^{2}=-\frac{1}{3}
and
\ell^{2}=0
and these curves generate the Mori cone of
S_{A_{5}}
. As
\Delta_{i}
is the unique curve with negative self-intersection, any automorphism of
S_{2A_{5}}
must fix
\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{2}
, and hence it follows that
\Aut(S_{2A_{5}})=\Aut(S_{2A_{5}},\Delta_{1}\cup\Delta_{2})
so
\Aut(X,D)\cong\Aut(D)
.
∎

Proposition 6.22
.

The morphism
\Psi
is quasi-finite, flat, and separated.

Proof.

First, we show quasi-finiteness of
\Psi
. Since
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is of finite type, we know that
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is quasi-compact which implies quasi-compactness of
\Psi
. By Proposition
6.21
we know that
\Psi
is representable. Thus by
[
Sta25
,
Tag 0G2M
and
Tag 06UA
]
it suffices to show that the induced map
|\Psi|:|\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}|\to|\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}|
on topological spaces has finite fibers. Indeed, we will show that
|\Psi|
is injective.

Let
[(X,\frac{2}{3}D)]\in|\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}|
. Then we will show that
|\Psi^{-1}(D)|
contains only one point. Let
[(X^{\prime},\frac{2}{3}D^{\prime})]\in|\Psi^{-1}(D)|
, i.e.
D\cong D^{\prime}
. If
\omega_{D}
is basepoint free, then Lemma
6.23
implies that both
X
and
X^{\prime}
are isomorphic to quadric surfaces and
D
is a
(2,3)
-complete intersection curve in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
. Since the embedding of
D\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{3}
is induced by
|\omega_{D}|
, it is unique up to projective transformations. Thus
D
is contained in a unique quadric surface, which implies that
(X,D)\cong(X^{\prime},D^{\prime})
. If
\omega_{D}
is not basepoint free, then Lemma
6.23
implies that
X\cong X^{\prime}\cong S_{2A_{5}}
. Thus the result follows from Lemma
6.20
.

Next, we show flatness of
\Psi
by applying the miracle flatness criterion. Since flatness is smooth local on the source-and-target, it suffices to show that the base change of
\Psi
to a smooth cover of
\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
by algebraic spaces is flat, and we may assume that each connected component of the cover is of finite type as
\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
is locally of finite type. Let
U\to\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
be a smooth morphism from an algebraic space
U
of finite type. Let
V:=U\times_{\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}}\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. Then
V
is an algebraic space by representability of
\Psi
from Proposition
6.21
. Since both algebraic stacks
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
and
\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
are smooth by Proposition
6.17
and
[
Sta25
,
Tag 0DZT
]
, we know that both
U
and
V
are smooth algebraic spaces of finite type. For simplicity, we may assume that
U
is connected and
V
is non-empty. Thus
U
dominates
\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
by irreducibility of
\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
(see Section
2.1
). Since
\Psi:\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}\to\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
is birational, we know that
\Psi_{U}:V\to U
is also birational between smooth irreducible algebraic spaces. We know that
\Psi_{U}
is quasi-finite as
\Psi
is quasi-finite. Thus
\Psi_{U}
is flat by miracle flatness
[
Sta25
,
Tag 00R4
]
. Therefore, the morphism
\Psi
is flat.

Finally, we show separatedness of
\Psi
by the valuative criterion. Suppose
(R,\mathfrak{m})
is a DVR essentially of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
with residue field
\kappa:=R/\mathfrak{m}
and fraction field
K:=\mathrm{Frac}(R)
. Let
f_{1},f_{2}:\Spec R\to\mathscr{W}^{\rm CY}
be two morphisms such that
\Psi\circ f_{1}=\Psi\circ f_{2}:\Spec R\to\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
. It suffices to show
f_{1}=f_{2}
. By pulling back the universal families over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
through
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
, we obtain families
(X_{1},\frac{2}{3}D_{1})
and
(X_{2},\frac{2}{3}D_{2})
of bpCY pairs over
\Spec R
such that
D_{1}\cong D_{2}
as
R
-schemes. Equivalently, it suffices to show
(X_{1},D_{1})\cong(X_{2},D_{2})
over
R
. Below we split into two cases.

If
\omega_{D_{i,\kappa}}
is basepoint free for every
i\in\{1,2\}
, by Lemma
6.23
we know that
X_{i,\kappa}
is a quadric surface for every
i
. By Lemma
6.8
we know that
X_{i,K}
is also a quadric surface for every
i
. In particular, the line bundle
\omega_{X_{i}}(D_{i})
is very ample over
R
for each
i
and induces a closed embedding
h_{i}:X_{i}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{3}_{R}
. Since
D_{1}\cong D_{2}
and
h_{i}|_{D_{i}}
is induced by
\omega_{D_{i}}
, there exists
g\in\mathrm{PGL}(4,R)
such that
D_{1}=g^{*}D_{2}
. Since every
(2,3)
-complete intersection curve in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
is contained in a unique quadric surface, we conclude that
X_{1}=g^{*}X_{2}
, which implies
(X_{1},D_{1})\cong(X_{2},D_{2})
over
R
.

If
\omega_{D_{i,\kappa}}
is not basepoint free for every
i\in\{1,2\}
, by Lemma
6.23
we know that
X_{i,\kappa}\cong S_{2A_{5}}
for every
i
. Since the stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is S-complete by Theorem
2.30
, we know that there exists
f:\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
such that
f_{1}=f|_{s\neq 0}
and
f_{2}=f|_{t\neq 0}
. Observe that the
S_{2A_{5}}
-locus in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is closed: as
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
contains only type I and II pairs (c.f.
[
ABB+23
, Proposition 8.18]
), any pair
(S_{2A_{5}},\frac{2}{3}C)
must be of type II and hence can only admit a specialization to a type II pair
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
by
[
ABB+23
, Proposition 8.8]
. The only type II polystable pairs in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
are given in Remark
6.15
, and
S_{2A_{5}}
cannot degenerate to either
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}
or
\mathbb{P}(1,2,3)\cup\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
as their double loci are normal crossing away from at most one point. Therefore,
S_{2A_{5}}
can only specialize to itself and hence the
S_{2A_{5}}
locus is closed. This implies that the the image of
f
is contained in
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
.
By assumption we know that
\Psi\circ f
factorizes into
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\to\Spec R\to\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
. Since
\Psi
is representable by Proposition
6.21
, it is also DM by
[
Sta25
,
Tag 050E
]
which implies that
f
also factorizes into
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\to\Spec R\to\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. Thus we conclude that
f_{1}=f_{2}
. This finishes the proof of separatedness of
\Psi
.
∎

The following lemma was used in the previous proof.

Lemma 6.23
.

Let
[(X,\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}(\mathbbm{k})
. Then
\omega_{D}
is basepoint free if and only if
X
is isomorphic to a quadric surface in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
.

Proof.

If
X
is a quadric surface, then
D\sim\mathcal{O}_{X}(3)
which implies that
\omega_{D}\cong\mathcal{O}_{D}(1)
is very ample and hence basepoint free. If
X\cong S_{2A_{5}}
, then we claim that
\omega_{D}
is not basepoint free. Indeed, a general small deformation
D^{\prime}
of
D
in
S_{2A_{5}}
is obtained by gluing two smooth genus
2
curves
D_{1}^{\prime}
and
D_{2}^{\prime}
at non-Weierstrass points
p_{1}\in D_{1}^{\prime}
and
p_{2}\in D_{2}^{\prime}
. Thus
\omega_{D^{\prime}}|_{D_{i}^{\prime}}\cong\omega_{D_{i}^{\prime}}(p_{i})
is not basepoint-free as
p_{i}
is not a Weierstrass point. Since basepoint-freeness is an open condition given that
h^{0}
of the canonical bundle is constant in this family, we know that
\omega_{D}
is not basepoint free.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.18
.

Since open immersion is smooth local on the source-and-target, by similar argument to the proof of Proposition
6.22
it suffices to show that
\Psi_{U}:V\to U
is an open immersion where
U\to\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
is a smooth morphism from a connected algebraic space
U
of finite type, where
V=U\times_{\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}}\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. Moreover, by Proposition
6.22
we know that both
U
and
V
are smooth irreducible algebraic spaces of finite type, and
\Psi_{U}
is quasi-finite, flat, separated, and birational. By Zariski’s main theorem for algebraic spaces
[
Sta25
,
Tag 082K
]
, we conclude that
\Psi_{U}
is an open immersion. Thus the proof is finished.
∎

7.
New models for the Hassett–Keel program

7.1.
New stacks and good moduli spaces

Definition 7.1
.

Recall that
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is the open substack of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{CY}}_{4}
parametrizing bpCY pairs
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)
such that
X
is either a quadric surface or
S_{2A_{5}}
. Let
\mathscr{W}:=\Psi(\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}})
be the corresponding open substack of
\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
by Theorem
6.18
. Denote two open substacks of
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
by
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{K}}:=\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
and
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}:=\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
. Denote by their images under
\Psi
by
\mathscr{W}^{-}:=\Psi(\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{K}})
and
\mathscr{W}^{+}:=\Psi(\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{KSBA}})
as open substacks of
\mathscr{W}
.

Definition 7.2
.

Recall from Definition
5.4
that
\phi:\mathscr{X}\to\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}
is a good moduli space morphism.
Define
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}:=\phi^{-1}(\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}\setminus(\Gamma\cup\{[C_{D}]\}))
and
\mathscr{V}_{2}:=\phi^{-1}(\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}\setminus(\Gamma\cup\{[C_{2A_{5}}]\}))
as open substacks of
\mathscr{X}
.

Since
\mathscr{V}=\phi^{-1}(\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}\setminus\Gamma)
, we have
\mathscr{V}=\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}
. Moreover,
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}\cap\mathscr{V}_{2}=\mathscr{U}\cap\mathscr{V}=\mathscr{X}^{\rm s}
. By Theorem
2.19
and Remark
2.20
, we have
\mathscr{V}\subset\mathscr{W}
.

Remark 7.3
.

By Theorem
2.19
, every curve in the Chow stable stack
\mathscr{X}^{\rm s}
lies on a normal quadric surface
Q
such that
(Q,\tfrac{2}{3}C)
is klt. In particular, we have
\mathscr{X}^{\rm s}\subset\mathscr{W}
and the preimage
\Psi^{-1}(\mathscr{X}^{\rm s})
is contained in each of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
, and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
by Proposition
2.34
.

Proposition 7.4
.

\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}
is isomorphic to a saturated open substack of the K-moduli stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
.

Proof.

As
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}
is contained in
\mathscr{V}
, it is contained in
\mathscr{W}
. Define
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{K}}
to be the preimage of
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}
under the forgetful map
\Psi:\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}\to\mathscr{W}
. By Theorem
2.19
, if
[(X,\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{K}}
, then
X
is a quadric of rank
\geq 3
and
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)
is klt for any
D
with
\phi([D])\neq[C_{2A_{5}}]
. In particular,
(X,(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)D)
is K-stable if
\phi([D])\neq[C_{2A_{5}}]
by Proposition
2.34
. By Lemma
6.1
, the polystable point
[C_{2A_{5}}]\in\mathscr{V}^{-}
has K-polystable preimage
[(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{2}{3}C_{2A_{5}})]\in\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{K}}
and thus any
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)
with
\phi([D])=[C_{2A_{5}}]
is K-semistable by openness of K-semistablity. Hence,
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{K}}\subset\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{K}}
. As
\Psi
is an open immersion,
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}\cong\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{K}}
an open substack of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
. Finally, by Lemma
6.8
and the polystability of
(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{2}{3}C_{2A_{5}})
from Lemma
6.1
, this substack is saturated.
∎

where the top row represents the moduli stacks and the bottom row the corresponding good moduli spaces. As
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{K}}
is saturated, the induced morphism
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{K}}
to its image in the good moduli space morphism
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}\to\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
is a good moduli space. Denote the image by
V_{1}^{\mathrm{K}}
.

Define
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{CY}}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
as the saturation of
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{K}}
, i.e. the preimage under the good moduli space morphism
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}\to\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
of the image of
V_{1}^{\mathrm{K}}
in
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
, which is open by Proposition
6.7
. Define
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
to be
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{CY}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
. These are saturated open substacks of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
, respectively. By Proposition
6.7
we know that
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{CY}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is an open substack. Finally, define
\mathscr{V}_{1}:=\Psi(\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{CY}})
and
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}:=\Psi(\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{KSBA}})
which are both open substacks of
\mathscr{W}
by Theorem
6.18
.

Proposition 7.6
.

We have a diagram
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{V}_{1}\hookleftarrow\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}
of open immersions that descends to good moduli spaces

\mathfrak{V}_{1}^{-}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathfrak{V}_{1}\xleftarrow{\psi_{1}^{+}}\mathfrak{V}_{1}^{+},

where
\psi_{1}^{+}
is a proper birational morphism. Furthermore, the exceptional locus of
\psi_{1}^{+}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier prime divisor birational to
\delta_{2}
that parameterizes curves as described in Proposition
6.6
which is contracted to the point
[C_{2A_{5}}^{\circ}]
by
\psi_{1}^{+}
.

Proof.

From Theorem
2.33
, there is a diagram of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}\hookleftarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
of open immersions that descends to good moduli spaces

\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}\rightarrow\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}\leftarrow\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}.

This induces the corresponding diagram of the saturated open substacks and their good moduli spaces

\lxSVG@closescope {

{{{{{}}{

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

By the isomorphisms
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{K}}\cong\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}
,
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{CY}}\cong\mathscr{V}_{1}
, and
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}\cong\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}
induced by the forgetful map
\Psi:\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}\to\mathscr{W}
, we have the existence of good moduli spaces and the diagram as claimed. It then suffices to verify the properties of the morphisms on good moduli spaces. By Theorem
6.16
,
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}
,
\mathscr{V}_{1}
, and
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}
are smooth and hence the good moduli spaces
\mathfrak{V}_{1}^{-}
,
\mathfrak{V}_{1}
, and
\mathfrak{V}_{1}^{+}
are normal. By Theorem
2.19
, the morphism
\psi_{1}^{-}:\mathfrak{V}_{1}^{-}\to\mathfrak{V}_{1}
is birational and an isomorphism on
\mathfrak{V}_{1}^{-}-\{[C_{2A_{5}}]\}
, hence finite, and therefore an isomorphism by Zariski’s Main Theorem. The morphism
\psi_{1}^{+}:\mathfrak{V}_{1}^{+}\to\mathfrak{V}_{1}
is an isomorphism away from the preimage of the complement of
\{[C_{2A_{5}}^{\circ}]\}
, and the exceptional locus of
\psi_{1}^{+}
is as described in Proposition
6.6
. From the description in the proposition, the curves parameterized by the exceptional locus are unions of two genus two hyperelliptic curves glued at a non-Weierstrass point. As the forgetful map
\Psi:\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}\to\mathscr{W}
is an isomorphism, the exceptional locus is birational to
\delta_{2}
and hence of codimension one. Next, the moduli space
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
has quotient singularities as it is the coarse space of the smooth Deligne–Mumford stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
and hence
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial by
[
KM98
, Proposition 5.15]
. This implies that the open subset
\mathfrak{V}_{1}^{+}\subset\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and therefore the codimension one exceptional locus of
\psi_{1}^{+}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier divisor. In fact, the exceptional locus of
\psi_{1}^{+}
is a prime divisor because it is dominated by the irreducible variety
\mathbb{P}(2,6,5,4,3)^{2}
by Proposition
6.6
.
Finally, the morphism
\mathfrak{V}_{1}^{+}\to\mathfrak{V}_{1}
is induced by base change of the proper morphism
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}\to\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
(see Theorem
2.33
) and is therefore proper.
∎

Proposition 7.7
.

\mathscr{V}_{2}
is isomorphic to a saturated open substack of the bpCY moduli stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. Moreover,
\mathscr{V}_{2}
is smooth.

Proof.

By definition,
\mathscr{V}_{2}\subset\mathscr{V}
so
\mathscr{V}_{2}\subset\mathscr{W}
. Let
\mathscr{V}_{2}^{\mathrm{CY}}
be its isomorphic preimage under the forgetful map
\Psi:\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}\to\mathscr{W}
. By Lemma
6.8
, being contained in a quadric is an open condition, so this is an open substack of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. Furthermore, by Remark
7.3
, all points of
\mathscr{V}_{2}
such that
\phi([D])\neq[C_{D}]
have polystable preimage in
\mathscr{V}_{2}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. If
[D]
is a point of
\mathscr{V}_{2}
such that
\phi([D])=[C_{D}]
, then the corresponding preimage
[(X,\frac{2}{3}D)]\in\mathscr{V}_{2}^{\mathrm{CY}}
admits a special degeneration to the polystable point
[(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},\frac{2}{3}C_{D})]
as described in Proposition
6.12
, which is a point of
\mathscr{V}_{2}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. By Lemma
6.8
,
\mathscr{V}_{2}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is saturated in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. Smoothness follows from Theorem
6.16
.
∎

Definition 7.8
.

Let
\mathscr{V}_{2}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}:=\mathscr{V}_{2}^{\mathrm{CY}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
be the corresponding saturated open substack in the KSBA moduli stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
. Let
\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}:=\Psi(\mathscr{V}_{2}^{\mathrm{KSBA}})
be its isomorphic image under the forgetful map.

Proposition 7.9
.

The open immersion
\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{V}_{2}
descends to a morphism of good moduli spaces
\psi_{2}^{+}:\mathfrak{V}_{2}^{+}\to\mathfrak{V}_{2}
which is a proper small birational morphism contracting the locus of curves in Lemma
6.11
to the point
[C_{D}]
.

Proof.

The existence of the good moduli spaces and morphism
\psi_{2}^{+}:\mathfrak{V}_{2}^{+}\to\mathfrak{V}_{2}
follows from the isomorphisms of
\mathscr{V}_{2}
and
\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}
with saturated open substacks of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
. This morphism is birational because it is an isomorphism on the dense klt locus and proper because it is constructed as the base change of the proper morphism
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}\to\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
. Finally, the exceptional locus of
\psi_{2}^{+}
is as described in Lemma
6.11
parameterizing elliptic triboroughs which get mapped to the single point
[(V(x_{0}x_{3}),\frac{2}{3}C_{D})]
under
\psi_{2}^{+}
, which has codimension three.
∎

Definition 7.10
.

For
\alpha\in[\frac{5}{9},\frac{2}{3})
, we define

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha):=\begin{cases}\mathscr{X}&\textrm{ if }\alpha=\frac{5}{9};\\
\mathscr{U}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}&\textrm{ if }\alpha\in(\frac{5}{9},\frac{19}{29});\\
\mathscr{U}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{1}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}&\textrm{ if }\alpha=\frac{19}{29};\\
\mathscr{U}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}&\textrm{ if }\alpha\in(\frac{19}{29},\frac{2}{3}).\end{cases}

A proper curve
C
over
\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}
is called
\alpha
-stable
if
C
is isomorphic to a curve represented by a
\mathbbm{k}^{\prime}
-point in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
.

Theorem 7.11
.

For each
\alpha\in[\frac{5}{9},\frac{2}{3})
, the stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
is smooth, algebraic, of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
with affine diagonal. There exists an open immersion
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}}
. Moreover, every
\alpha
-stable curve has an ample canonical bundle.

Proof.

From the definitions above we know that for each
\alpha\in[\frac{5}{9},\frac{2}{3})
, the stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
admits an open immersion into
\mathscr{X}\cup\mathscr{W}
. By Theorems
5.1
and
6.18
, we know that both
\mathscr{X}
and
\mathscr{W}
are smooth open substacks of
\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}}
of finite type over
\mathbbm{k}
with affine diagonal, which implies the first and second statements on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
. For the last statement, notice that if
[C]\in\mathscr{X}
then
C
has ample and basepoint free canonical bundle by definition; if
[C]\in\mathscr{W}
then there exists a surface
X
containing
C
as a Cartier divisor such that
[(X,\frac{2}{3}C)]\in\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
, which implies that
\omega_{C}^{\otimes 2}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}2(K_{X}+C)|_{C}\sim-K_{X}|_{C}
is ample by adjunction.
∎

Theorem 7.12
.

For each
\alpha\in[\frac{5}{9},\frac{2}{3})
, the stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
admits a proper good moduli space which we denote by
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
. Moreover, we have a diagram of open immersions of stacks

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{5}{9})\hookleftarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{5}{9},\tfrac{19}{29})\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{19}{29})\hookleftarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{19}{29},\tfrac{2}{3}),

which induces a wall crossing diagram of birational morphisms:

\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}\cong\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{5}{9})\xleftarrow{j_{5}^{+}}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{5}{9},\tfrac{19}{29})\xrightarrow[\cong]{j_{4}^{-}}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{19}{29})\xleftarrow{j_{4}^{+}}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{19}{29},\tfrac{2}{3}).

The wall crossing morphisms have the following properties.

(1)

The exceptional locus of
j_{5}^{+}
parameterizes elliptic triboroughs as described in the second half of Proposition
6.12
, which get mapped to the point
[C_{D}]
in
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{5}{9})
, the hyperelliptic curves parameterized by
\mathfrak{H}_{4}^{+}
which get mapped to
[R_{\mathrm{hyp}}]
in
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{5}{9})
, and the curves in Proposition
2.36
with
h_{4}(x,y)\neq 0
which get mapped to the appropriate point
[C_{A,B}]
in
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{5}{9})
.

(2)

The isomorphism
j_{4}^{-}
identifies the point
[C_{2A_{5}}]
with the point
[C_{2A_{5}}^{\circ}]
.

(3)

The exceptional locus of
j_{4}^{+}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier prime divisor as a birational transform of
\delta_{2}
and parameterizes unions of genus 2 hyperelliptic curves glued at a non-Weierstrass point with at worst
A_{4}
singularities as described in Proposition
6.6
, which get mapped to the point
[C_{2A_{5}}^{\circ}]
by
j_{4}^{+}
.

Proof.

When
\alpha=\frac{5}{9}
, the existence of a proper good moduli space isomorphic to
\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}
follows from Theorem
5.1
. Thus we shall focus on showing the existence of proper good moduli spaces for
\alpha\in(\frac{5}{9},\frac{2}{3})
.
By Propositions
7.6
,
7.9
and Corollary
5.11
, each open substack
\mathscr{U}^{+}
,
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}
,
\mathscr{V}_{1}
,
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}
,
\mathscr{V}_{2}
, and
\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}
of
\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{Gor}}
admits a good moduli space. Furthermore, their pairwise intersections are the Chow stable locus
\mathscr{X}^{\rm s}
, which is saturated, and thus the induced good moduli spaces agree on their intersections. Therefore, we may construct a good moduli space
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
of their union by gluing the associated good moduli space morphisms by Theorem
2.10
.

The diagram on open immersions of stacks
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
follows directly from Definition
7.10
and definitions of relevant stacks
\mathscr{X}
,
\mathscr{U}^{+}
,
\mathscr{V}_{1}
,
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\pm}
,
\mathscr{V}_{2}
, and
\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}
. By Theorem
2.7
this descends to a diagram for good moduli spaces
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
.

Next, we show properness of
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
, which will follow by properness of
\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}
and properness of the wall crossing morphisms in Theorem
5.6
, Proposition
7.6
, and Proposition
7.9
and two general remarks on properness:

(i)

If
Y
is proper and
X\to Y
is a proper morphism, then
X
is proper
[
Sta25
,
Tag 01W3
]
.

(ii)

If
X
is proper and
X\to Y
is a proper surjective morphism such that
Y
is locally of finite type, then
Y
is proper
[
Sta25
,
Tag 03GN
,
Tag 09MQ
]
.

The wall crossing morphisms for
\mathfrak{V}_{i}^{\pm}
are proper by Propositions
7.6
,
7.9
, and the wall crossing morphism
\mathfrak{U}^{+}\to\mathfrak{U}
is proper as it is the base change of the proper morphism
\mathfrak{X}^{+}\to\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}
from the proof of Theorem
5.6
. These morphisms are surjective on
\mathbbm{k}
-points by construction, so surjective by
[
Sta25
,
Tag 0487
]
. The good moduli spaces
\mathfrak{V}_{1}^{-}
,
\mathfrak{V}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{V}_{1}^{+}
,
\mathfrak{V}_{2}
, and
\mathfrak{V}_{2}^{+}
are of finite type as they are isomorphic to subspaces of the projective varieties
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}
,
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
, and
\overline{M}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
and
\mathfrak{U}
,
\mathfrak{U}^{+}
are of finite type as they are contained in the proper good moduli spaces
\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}
,
\mathfrak{X}^{+}
. Therefore, as each
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
is obtained by wall crossing from the proper moduli space
\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}
, each
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
is proper.

The description of the exceptional loci follows from Propositions
7.6
,
7.9
and Corollary
5.13
.
∎

Theorem 7.13
.

If
\alpha\in(\frac{19}{29},\frac{2}{3})
, then the stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
(resp. the good moduli space
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
) is isomorphic to the stack of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
(resp. the good moduli space
\overline{M}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
) defined in
[
AFSvdW17
]
. In particular, the wall crossing diagram in Theorem
7.12
can be extended to

\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}\cong\overline{M}_{4}(\tfrac{5}{9})\xleftarrow{j_{5}^{+}}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{5}{9},\tfrac{19}{29})\xrightarrow[\cong]{j_{4}^{-}}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{19}{29})\xleftarrow{j_{4}^{+}}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\tfrac{19}{29},\tfrac{2}{3})\cong\overline{M}_{4}(\tfrac{2}{3}-\epsilon)\xrightarrow{j_{3}^{-}}\overline{M}_{4}(\tfrac{2}{3}).

Proof.

Recall that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)=\mathscr{U}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}
. We will show that there is an open immersion
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
that takes closed points to closed points and apply
[
Alp13
, Proposition 6.4]
to prove the isomorphism of stacks.

First, consider
\mathscr{U}^{+}
. By Propositions
5.7
and
5.9
,
\mathscr{U}^{+}
admits an open immersion to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
that takes closed points to closed points.

Now, consider
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}
. By definition,
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}
is isomorphic to the open substack
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}\subset\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
. We claim that the forgetful map
\Psi:\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}\to\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
has image contained in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
. Indeed, from Definition
2.11
, it suffices to verify that the curves
D
in the pairs
[(X,D)]\in\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
have the required properties. This holds for all
D
in the Chow stable locus
\mathscr{X}^{\rm s}
by Remark
2.20
. If
(X,D)
satisfies that
D\not\in\mathscr{X}^{\rm s}
, then
X\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}
or
S_{2A_{5}}
and the pair admits a special degeneration to
(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}},C_{D})
or
(S_{2A_{5}},C_{2A_{5}}^{\circ})
, respectively. Such pairs also have images contained in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
: the singularity condition holds by Proposition
6.6
and Proposition
6.12
, and the conditions on tails and chains hold from the geometry of
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}
and
S_{2A_{5}}
. Indeed, on
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}
, the curve
D=D_{1}\cup D_{2}
is a union of two arithmetic genus one components (one on each copy of
\mathbb{P}^{2}
) meeting transversally at three distinct points, so by construction cannot contain an irreducible genus two curve and thus can contain no Weierstrass chains. An elliptic tail is necessarily irreducible, so if it occurred on
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}
, would have to be either
D_{1}
or
D_{2}
, but then would meet the rest of the curve at three points and thus not a tail. Similarly, an elliptic chain could not be equal to
D_{1}
or
D_{2}
, so would necessarily be the union of two rational curves meeting in an
A_{3}
singularity, with one rational curve on each component
\mathbb{P}^{2}
. This contradicts the components
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
meeting transversally. Therefore, there are no elliptic or Weierstrass chains or tails on
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}
.
On
S_{2A_{5}}
, a curve
D=C_{1}\cup C_{2}
where each
C_{i}
is an arithmetic genus 2 curve and the intersection
C_{1}\cap C_{2}
is a non-Weierstrass point of each
C_{i}
. By construction, each
C_{i}
is the strict transform of a curve of degree 6 on
\mathbb{P}(1,1,3)
not passing through the singular point, and this implies that if
C_{i}
is reducible, it is the union of at most two arithmetic genus
0
components (yet their union meets
C_{3-i}
at only one point). Therefore, there can be no elliptic or Weierstrass chains or tails. In particular, the forgetful map
\Psi:\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}\to\mathcal{C}urves_{4}^{\mathrm{lci}+}
has image contained in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
.

Furthermore, on
\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}
, the forgetful map is an open immersion by Theorem
6.18
and therefore the inclusion
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
is an open immersion. To verify it takes closed points to closed points, observe that
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}\cap\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}
is contained in
\mathscr{U}^{+}
and any point of
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}\cap\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}
is stable and its closure in
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}
is equivalent to its closure in
\mathscr{U}^{+}
. These points are then closed in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
by Proposition
5.9
. The remaining curves in
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}
are either curves on
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}
or
S_{2A_{5}}
. The curves on
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{0}}\cup\mathbb{P}^{2}_{x_{3}}
have at worst
A_{3}
singularities, hence are closed in the DM stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon)
(c.f. Lemma
2.14
). As these curves are contained in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
, by local VGIT they are closed in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
. For curves on
S_{2A_{5}}
, write
C=C_{1}\cup C_{2}
as the union of the arithmetic genus 2 curves on each component of
S_{2A_{5}}
. By Proposition
6.6
,
C
has at worst
A_{4}
singularities. If both components have at worst
A_{3}
singularities, then the same argument applies to show
[C]
is closed in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon)
. Assume then that
C_{1}
has an
A_{4}
singularity. By construction,
C_{1}
is an
\alpha_{\frac{2}{3}}
atom. The second component
C_{2}
is either an
\alpha_{\frac{2}{3}}
atom if it also has an
A_{4}
singularity, or is a curve in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{2,1}(\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon)
. In the latter case, by Remark
5.10
,
[C_{2}]
is closed in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{2,1}(\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon)
. Therefore, in either case,
C
is closed in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
by
[
AFSvdW17
, Definition 2.21, Theorem 2.22]
.

Finally, by gluing together
\mathscr{U}^{+}
and
\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}
along the Chow stable locus
\mathscr{X}^{\rm s}
, we conclude the existence of an open immersion
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
sending closed points to closed points. This implies that the induced map between the proper good moduli spaces is proper and injective and hence finite, so by
[
Alp13
, Proposition 6.4]
,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
is finite. As it was an open immersion, we conclude
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)\cong\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)
by Zariski’s Main Theorem.
∎

7.2.
Projectivity

For
\alpha\in(\frac{5}{9},\frac{2}{3})
, denote by
\varphi_{\alpha}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}\dashrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
the birational contraction. Denote by
\lambda_{\alpha}:=(\varphi_{\alpha})_{*}\lambda
and
\delta_{i,\alpha}:=(\varphi_{\alpha})_{*}\delta_{i}
. From the construction, we know that
\varphi_{\alpha}
always contracts
\delta_{1}
, and it contracts
\delta_{2}
if and only if
\alpha\in(\frac{5}{9},\frac{19}{29}]
.

Definition 7.14
.

For each
\alpha\in(\frac{5}{9},\frac{2}{3})\cap\mathbb{Q}
, we define a
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}
on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
as

\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}:=\begin{cases}13\lambda_{\alpha}-(2-\alpha)\delta_{0,\alpha}&\textrm{ if }\alpha\in(\frac{5}{9},\frac{19}{29}];\\
13\lambda_{\alpha}-(2-\alpha)(\delta_{0,\alpha}+\delta_{2,\alpha})&\textrm{ if }\alpha\in(\frac{19}{29},\frac{2}{3}).\end{cases}

Theorem 7.15
.

(1)

For any
\alpha\in(\frac{5}{9},\frac{2}{3})\cap\mathbb{Q}
,
\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and ample on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
.

(2)

For any
\alpha\in[\frac{5}{9},\frac{2}{3})\cap\mathbb{Q}
, we have

(7.1)

\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)\cong\overline{M}_{4}(\alpha):=\Proj R(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4},K_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}}+\alpha\delta).

Proposition 7.16
.

Theorem
7.15
(1) holds if
\alpha\in(\frac{19}{29},\frac{2}{3})\cap\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

By
[
AFS17b
]
we know that there exists a sufficiently small
\epsilon_{0}\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
such that
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and ample for every
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Q}
. Thus we may assume that
\alpha\in(\frac{19}{29},\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon_{0})\cap\mathbb{Q}
. It suffices to show
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}:=13\lambda_{\alpha}-(2-\frac{19}{29})(\delta_{0,\alpha}+\delta_{2,\alpha})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and nef on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
, as
\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}
is a strictly convex combination of
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon_{0}}
and
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}
.

Next, we adopt the notation from
[
ADL23
]
of
\eta
and
\xi
on the VGIT quotients (see also Section
2.4
). By
[
CMJL14
, Proof of Theorem 7.1]
the pull-back of these line bundles to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}
are given by

(7.2)

\phi_{t}^{*}(4s\eta+4\xi)=(34s-33)\lambda-(4s-4)\delta_{0}-(14s-15)\delta_{1}-(18s-21)\delta_{2}.

Here
\phi_{t}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}\dashrightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{t}=W^{\rm ss}(N_{t})\sslash\mathrm{PGL}(4)
is the birational contraction and
s=\frac{1}{t}
. Denote by

\phi^{\rm c}_{\alpha}:=\phi_{\frac{2}{3}}\circ\varphi_{\alpha}^{-1}=j_{5}^{+}\circ(j_{4}^{-})^{-1}\circ j_{4}^{+}:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)\to\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}.

Then by Theorem
7.12
we know that
\phi_{\alpha}^{\rm c}
is a birational morphism.
Since
\frac{3}{2}\eta+\xi=\frac{3}{2}(\eta+\frac{2}{3}\xi)
is a positive multiple of the GIT polarization on
\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}
, we know that

(7.3)

(\phi_{\alpha}^{\rm c})^{*}(4\cdot\tfrac{3}{2}\eta+4\xi)=18\lambda_{\alpha}-2\delta_{0,\alpha}-6\delta_{2,\alpha}=2(9\lambda_{\alpha}-\delta_{0,\alpha}-3\delta_{2,\alpha})

is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier, big and nef on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
. On the other hand, by
[
AFS17b
]
we know that
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{2}{3}}^{-}:=13\lambda_{\alpha}-(2-\frac{2}{3})(\delta_{0,\alpha}+\delta_{2,\alpha})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier, big and nef on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
. Thus

(7.4)

\frac{1}{3}(9\lambda_{\alpha}-\delta_{0,\alpha}-3\delta_{2,\alpha})+2\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{2}{3}}^{-}=29\lambda_{\alpha}-3\delta_{0,\alpha}-\frac{11}{3}\delta_{2,\alpha}=\frac{29}{13}\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}-\frac{2}{3}\delta_{2,\alpha}

is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier, big and nef on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
. Since
\delta_{2,\alpha}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier by Theorem
7.12
(3), so is
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}
. Thus to show nefness of
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}
, it suffices to show nefness of its restriction
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}|_{\delta_{2,\alpha}}
. Indeed, we will show that this restriction is trivial.

For any
t\in(0,\frac{2}{3})
, consider the birational map
\phi_{t}\circ\varphi_{\alpha}^{-1}:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)\dashrightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{t}
as the composition of the birational morphism
\phi_{\alpha}^{\rm c}
and the birational map
\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}\dashrightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{t}
. Since the latter map is an isomorphism near
[C_{2A_{5}}]
, we know that
\phi_{t}\circ\varphi_{\alpha}^{-1}
is regular near
\delta_{2,\alpha}
which contracts
\delta_{2,\alpha}
to a point. Thus by (
7.2
)

\left.\left((\phi_{t}\circ\varphi_{\alpha}^{-1})^{*}(4s\eta+4\xi)\right)\right|_{\delta_{2,\alpha}}=\left.\left((34s-33)\lambda_{\alpha}-(4s-4)\delta_{0,\alpha}-(18s-21)\delta_{2,\alpha}\right)\right|_{\delta_{2,\alpha}}

is trivial for any
t\in(0,\frac{2}{3})
. Since the above divisor is linear in
s
, we know that it is trivial for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
. In particular, we can choose
s=\frac{17}{14}
which yields

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\left.\left((34\cdot\tfrac{17}{14}-33)\lambda_{\alpha}-(4\cdot\tfrac{17}{14}-4)\delta_{0,\alpha}-(18\cdot\tfrac{17}{14}-21)\delta_{2,\alpha}\right)\right|_{\delta_{2,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\left.\left(\tfrac{58}{7}\lambda_{\alpha}-\tfrac{6}{7}(\delta_{0,\alpha}+\delta_{2,\alpha})\right)\right|_{\delta_{2,\alpha}}=\tfrac{58}{91}\left.\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}\right|_{\delta_{2,\alpha}}.

This shows triviality of
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}|_{\delta_{2,\alpha}}
and concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.17
.

Theorem
7.15
(1) holds if
\alpha=\frac{19}{29}
.

Proof.

We first show
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartierness of
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}
.
Pick some
\alpha^{\prime}\in(\frac{19}{29},\frac{2}{3})\cap\mathbb{Q}
. Then we have the birational morphism
j_{4}^{+}:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha^{\prime})\to\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\frac{19}{29})
which contracts
\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}
to a point
p
and is isomorphic elsewhere. From the proof of Proposition
7.16
we know that
\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}
and the following two
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors

\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{2}{3}}^{-}=13\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}-\tfrac{4}{3}(\delta_{0,\alpha^{\prime}}+\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}})\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}=13\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}-\tfrac{39}{29}(\delta_{0,\alpha^{\prime}}+\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}})

are all
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier. Thus we have that
\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}
and
\delta_{0,\alpha^{\prime}}
are both
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier. This implies that both
\lambda_{\alpha}
and
\delta_{0,\alpha}
are
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier away from
p
.

Similar to the proof of Proposition
7.16
, for each
t\in(0,\frac{2}{3})
the birational map
\phi_{t}\circ\varphi_{\alpha}^{-1}:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\frac{19}{29})\dashrightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{t}
is an isomorphism near
p
which sends
p
to
[C_{2A_{5}}]
. Thus

(\phi_{t}\circ\varphi_{\alpha}^{-1})^{*}(4s\eta+4\xi)=(34s-33)\lambda_{\alpha}-(4s-4)\delta_{0,\alpha}

is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier near
p
for every
t\in(0,\frac{2}{3})
. Thus both
\lambda_{\alpha}
and
\delta_{0,\alpha}
are
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier near
p
. This concludes the proof of
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartierness of
\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}
.

Next, we show nefness of
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}
. Clearly,
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}-(j_{4}^{+})^{*}\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}
only supports on
\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}
whose restriction to
\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}
is trivial. Hence the negativity lemma
[
KM98
, Lemma 3.39]
implies

(7.5)

\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}=(j_{4}^{+})^{*}\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}.

Thus the nefness of
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}
follows from the nefness of
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}
as shown in the proof of Proposition
7.16
.

Finally, we show ampleness of
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}
. By the Nakai-Moishezon criterion, it suffices to show that for every positive dimensional closed integral algebraic subspace
Z\subset\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
, the restriction
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}|_{Z}
is big. Since
Z
is not contained in the exceptional locus of
j_{4}^{+}
which is a single point
p
, the birational transform
Z^{\prime}
of
Z
under
j_{4}^{+}
is a closed integral subvariety of
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha^{\prime})
. It suffices to show that
(j_{4}^{+})^{*}\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}|_{Z^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}|_{Z^{\prime}}
is big. Similar to (
7.4
), for
0<\epsilon\ll 1
we can pick positive numbers
a:=\frac{1-87\epsilon}{3(1-9\epsilon)}
and
b:=\frac{2}{1-9\epsilon}
such that

(7.6)

a(9\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}-\delta_{0,\alpha^{\prime}}-3\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}})+b\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}=\tfrac{29}{13}\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}-2a\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}.

From the proof of Proposition
7.16
we know that
9\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}-\delta_{0,\alpha^{\prime}}-3\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}
(resp.
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon}
) is nef (resp. ample). Thus (
7.6
) implies that
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}-\frac{26}{29}a\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}
is ample. As a result, the restriction
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}|_{Z^{\prime}}-\frac{26}{29}a\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}|_{Z^{\prime}}
is also ample. Since
Z^{\prime}
is not contained in
\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}
and
a>0
, the
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\frac{26}{29}a\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}|_{Z^{\prime}}
is effective. Therefore,
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{+}|_{Z^{\prime}}
is ample plus effective and hence big. This finishes the proof of ampleness.
∎

Proposition 7.18
.

Theorem
7.15
(1) holds if
\alpha\in(\frac{5}{9},\frac{19}{29})\cap\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

We define two
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
as

\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{-}:=(j_{4}^{-})^{*}\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}=13\lambda_{\alpha}-(2-\tfrac{19}{29})\delta_{0,\alpha}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{5}{9}}^{+}:=13\lambda_{\alpha}-(2-\tfrac{5}{9})\delta_{0,\alpha}.

Since
j_{4}^{-}
is an isomorphism by Theorem
7.12
, Proposition
7.17
implies that
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{-}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and ample.
Similar to the proofs of Proposition
7.16
and
7.17
,

\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{5}{9}}^{+}=\tfrac{13}{18}(j_{5}^{+})^{*}(4\cdot\tfrac{3}{2}\eta+4\xi)

is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier, big and nef. Since
\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}
is a strict convex combination of
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{19}{29}}^{-}
and
\mathfrak{L}_{\frac{5}{9}}^{+}
, we conclude that it is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and ample.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.15
.

Part (1) follows directly from the combination of Propositions
7.16
,
7.17
, and
7.18
. Thus we focus on part (2) for the rest of the proof. If
\alpha=\frac{5}{9}
, then (2) follows from Theorems
2.22
and
7.12
. Thus we may assume
\alpha\in(\frac{5}{9},\frac{2}{3})\cap\mathbb{Q}
from now on.

We start from the case where
\alpha\in(\frac{19}{29},\frac{2}{3})
.
From
[
AFSvdW17
]
and Theorem
7.13
we know that the birational map
\overline{M}_{4}^{\rm ps}\dashrightarrow\overline{M}_{4}(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)\cong\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
is an isomorphism in codimension
1
and at the center of
\delta_{1}
under the divisorial contraction
\overline{M}_{4}\to\overline{M}_{4}^{\rm ps}
. Thus by
[
HH09
, Lemma 4.1]
we have

K_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}}+\alpha\delta=\varphi_{\alpha}^{*}\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}+(9-11\alpha)\delta_{1}.

Here we use the canonical bundle formula
K_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}}=13\lambda-2\delta
(see e.g.
[
HM98
, Section 3.E]
or
[
Mor10
, 1.45]
). Since
\alpha<\frac{9}{11}
by assumption and
\delta_{1}
is
\varphi_{\alpha}
-exceptional, (
7.1
) follows from the ampleness of
\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}
.

Finally we study the case where
\alpha\in(\frac{5}{9},\frac{19}{29}]
.
Pick some
\alpha^{\prime}\in(\frac{19}{29},\frac{2}{3})
. According to Theorem
7.12
, let
j:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha^{\prime})\to\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
be the birational morphism that only contracts
\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}
and is isomorphic elsewhere (in fact,
j
is
j_{4}^{+}
if
\alpha=\frac{19}{29}
or
(j_{4}^{-})^{-1}\circ j_{4}^{+}
otherwise). Notice that
\phi_{\alpha}^{\rm c}:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha)\to\mathfrak{X}^{\rm c}
is a birational morphism by Theorem
7.12
. Thus (
7.3
) implies that

(7.7)

9\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}-\delta_{0,\alpha^{\prime}}-3\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}=(\phi_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{\rm c})^{*}(3\lambda+2\xi)=j^{*}(\phi_{\alpha}^{\rm c})^{*}(3\lambda+2\xi)=9j^{*}\lambda_{\alpha}-j^{*}\delta_{0,\alpha}.

Moreover, by (
7.5
) we know that

(7.8)

29\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}-3\delta_{0,\alpha^{\prime}}-3\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}=29j^{*}\lambda_{\alpha}-3j^{*}\delta_{0,\alpha}.

Combining (
7.7
) and (
7.8
) yields

j^{*}\lambda_{\alpha}=\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}+3\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}},\qquad j^{*}\delta_{0,\alpha}=\delta_{0,\alpha^{\prime}}+30\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle j^{*}\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=j^{*}(13\lambda_{\alpha}-(2-\alpha)\delta_{0,\alpha})

\displaystyle=13\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}-(2-\alpha)\delta_{0,\alpha^{\prime}}+(39-30(2-\alpha))\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=13\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}-(2-\alpha)(\delta_{0,\alpha^{\prime}}+\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}})-(19-29\alpha)\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}.

Since the center of
\delta_{1}
on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{4}(\alpha^{\prime})
generically parameterizes irreducible curves with an
A_{2}
-singularity, it is not contained in
\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}
which implies
\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{*}\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}=\delta_{2}
. Again by
[
HH09
, Lemma 4.1]
we have

\displaystyle K_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}}+\alpha\delta

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{*}(13\lambda_{\alpha^{\prime}}-(2-\alpha)(\delta_{0,\alpha^{\prime}}+\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}))+(9-11\alpha)\delta_{1}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{*}j^{*}\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}+(19-29\alpha)\varphi_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{*}\delta_{2,\alpha^{\prime}}+(9-11\alpha)\delta_{1}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\alpha}^{*}\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}+(19-29\alpha)\delta_{2}+(9-11\alpha)\delta_{1}.

Since
\alpha<\frac{19}{29}<\frac{9}{11}
by assumption and
\delta_{1}
,
\delta_{2}
are both
\varphi_{\alpha}
-exceptional, (
7.1
) follows from the ampleness of
\mathfrak{L}_{\alpha}
.
∎

7.3.
Proofs of Theorems
1.1
and
1.2

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

The properties of the stacks
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
follows directly from Theorem
7.11
.
The existence of projective good moduli spaces and all parts of the diagram except the right-most square follow from Theorems
7.12
and
7.15
. The right-most square follows from Proposition
5.3
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

This follows from Theorem
1.1
, Theorem
7.13
, and the previous works
[
Fed12
,
CMJL12
,
CMJL14
]
for the Hassett–Keel models with
\alpha<\frac{5}{9}
and the works
[
HH09
,
HH13
,
AFS17a
]
for the Hassett–Keel models with
\alpha>\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon
.
∎

Chow semistable 1-cycles in
\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
of degree
2g-2
in the closure of the locus parameterizing smooth canonical curves of genus
g

Definition
2.15

\mathscr{X}_{t}

VGIT quotient stack of
(2,3)
complete intersections in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
of slope
t

Definition
2.17

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}

slc pairs
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)
admitting a
\mathbb{Q}
-Gorenstein smoothing to
(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1},\frac{2}{3}C_{3,3})
, for
C_{3,3}
a smooth
(3,3)
curve

Definition
2.29

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{K}}

pairs
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}(\mathbbm{k})
such that
(X,(\frac{2}{3}-\epsilon)D)
is K-semistable for
0<\epsilon\ll 1

Definition
2.31

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{KSBA}}

pairs
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}(\mathbbm{k})
such that
(X,(\frac{2}{3}+\epsilon)D)
is slc for
0<\epsilon\ll 1

3.15

\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}

curves
C\in\mathcal{C}urves_{g}^{\mathrm{Gor}}(\mathbbm{k})
such that
\omega_{C}
is ample and basepoint free

Definition
4.1

\overline{\mathscr{X}}_{\!g}

curves in the closure of
\mathcal{M}_{g}
in
\mathcal{C}urves_{g,1}
, i.e. smoothable Gorenstein genus
g
curves with
\omega_{C}
ample and basepoint free

Definition
4.2

\mathscr{X}_{g}

smoothable Gorenstein genus
g
curves with
\omega_{C}
ample and basepoint free whose Hilbert–Chow image is a Chow-semistable 1-cycle in
\mathbb{P}^{g-1}

Definition
4.2

\mathscr{X}_{g}^{\circ}

smoothable lci genus
g
curves with
\omega_{C}
ample and basepoint free whose Hilbert–Chow image is a Chow-semistable 1-cycle in
\mathbb{P}^{g-1}
whose Chow polystable degeneration is either a doubled rational normal curve or the cycle theoretic image of a reduced lci curve with very ample canonical divisor

Definition-Proposition
4.9
; a smooth open substack of
\mathscr{X}_{g}
that admits a good moduli space (if
g=4
,
\mathscr{X}_{4}^{\circ}
will be all of
\mathscr{X}_{4}
)

\mathscr{X}^{\rm c}:=\mathscr{X}_{4}^{\rm c}

Chow semistable 1-cycles in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
of degree
6
in the closure of the locus parameterizing smooth canonical curves of genus
g

Definition
2.15

\mathscr{X}:=\mathscr{X}_{4}=\mathscr{X}_{4}^{\circ}

curves in
\mathscr{X}_{g}
for
g=4
, i.e. smoothable Gorenstein genus
4
curves with
\omega_{C}
ample and basepoint free whose Hilbert–Chow image is a Chow-semistable 1-cycle in
\mathbb{P}^{3}

Definition
4.2
, Definition-Proposition
4.9
, Lemma
5.2

\mathscr{U}

curves in
\mathscr{X}
whose Chow 1-cycle does not admit special degenerations to
[C_{2A_{5}}]
or
[C_{D}]

Definition
5.4

\mathscr{V}

curves in
\mathscr{X}
whose Chow 1-cycle does not admit special degenerations to
[C_{A,B}]
for any
(A,B)\in\mathbbm{k}^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}

Definition
5.4

\mathscr{X}^{+}

curves
C\in\mathscr{X}(\mathbbm{k})
such that either
C
has
A_{\leq 4}
singularities or
C\in\mathscr{V}

Definition
5.5

\mathscr{U}^{+}

curves
C\in\mathscr{X}^{+}(\mathbbm{k})\cap\mathscr{U}(\mathbbm{k})

Definition
5.5

\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}

pairs
(X,\frac{2}{3}D)\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}(\mathbbm{k})
such that
X
is a quadric or
S_{2A_{5}}

\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}

curves in
\mathscr{V}
whose Chow 1-cycle does not admit special degenerations to
[C_{D}]
, or curves in the pre-image of
\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{c}}\setminus(\Gamma\cup\{[C_{D}]\})
the good moduli space morphism
\mathscr{X}\to\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{c}}

Definition
7.2

\mathscr{V}_{1}

denoting by
\Psi:\mathscr{W}^{\mathrm{CY}}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}\to\mathscr{W}
the forgetful map, curves in the image of the saturation of
\Psi^{-1}(\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-})
under
\Psi

Definition
7.5

\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}

curves in
\mathscr{V}_{1}(\mathbbm{k})\cap\mathscr{W}^{+}(\mathbbm{k})

\mathscr{V}_{1}
whose preimage in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}^{\mathrm{CY}}
is a KSBA stable pair

Definition

the good moduli space morphism
\mathscr{X}\to\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{c}}

Definition

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(\alpha)
for
\alpha\in[\frac{5}{9},\frac{2}{3})

\mathbbm{k}
-points of

\mathscr{U}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{1}^{-}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}&\textrm{ if }\alpha\in(\frac{5}{9},\frac{19}{29})\\
\mathscr{U}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{1}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}&\textrm{ if }\alpha=\frac{19}{29}\\
\mathscr{U}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{1}^{+}\cup\mathscr{V}_{2}^{+}&\textrm{ if }\alpha\in(\frac{19}{29},\frac{2}{3})\end{cases}

7.10
; this stack admits
\overline{M}_{4}(\alpha)
